The only persistent bytes of a media object anywhere on the device are its E2E ciphertext blobs, byte-identical to relay blobs, and its rows (§13.6): the blobs live in `noBackupFilesDir/m/` and, for a bounded time, in SQLCipher's Phase 7 tables (inbound in `inbox_blob.ciphertext` until `MEDIA-INGEST` consumes them, at most 16 MiB of `M_0` at once; outbound in `outbox_op` until each op's last store, at most 4 MiB; and in the WAL frames of both until the next checkpoint; §10.4). Plaintext (content, previews, decoded pixels, decrypted chunks, captions outside `body_env`) is never written: it exists only in RAM. GHOST zeroises the native buffers it owns and erases its bitmaps before recycling them (§10.5); RAM it does not own (freed JVM arrays such as Media3's sample buffers, GPU textures, the compositor's and the codecs' buffers) is not overwritten and may keep plaintext until reused (declared, L9-M7).

### 10.2 Media3 configuration

- `GhostMediaDataSource` implements Media3's `DataSource` over one open reader: `open` returns the content length; `read(buffer, offset, length)` maps the position to a chunk, fetches its four fragment files, calls `readerChunk(mode = read)` into a direct buffer and copies into Media3's array; at most two decrypted chunks are cached in native memory and zeroised on eviction and at `close`.
- The player is `ExoPlayer.Builder(context, ghostRenderersFactory, ProgressiveMediaSource.Factory(ghostDataSourceFactory, fmp4OnlyExtractorsFactory))` with `setUsePlatformDiagnostics(false)` and a `DefaultLoadControl` of 5–15 s and at most 8 MiB of buffer (Media3's defaults buffer 50 s and up to about 125 MiB of video). The extractors factory returns only `FragmentedMp4Extractor` with `FLAG_READ_MFRA_FOR_SEEK_MAP`; the renderers factory builds exactly one `MediaCodecVideoRenderer` and one `MediaCodecAudioRenderer` (G-M3's configuration rule, S0-21). **Why the renderers factory is not cosmetic (S0 V-M14):** `ExoPlayer.Builder(context, mediaSourceFactory)` uses Media3's `DefaultRenderersFactory`, whose `ImageRenderer` decodes images with `BitmapFactory` in the main process (`BitmapFactoryImageDecoder`), and R8 keeps it in the release dex; with GHOST's factory R8 removes `ImageRenderer`, `BitmapFactoryImageDecoder`, `TextRenderer`, `MetadataRenderer` and `DefaultRenderersFactory`, which G-M10 then checks.
- `androidx.media3.common.util.Log.setLogLevel(Log.LOG_LEVEL_OFF)` at process start (an `@UnstableApi` public call).
- No `MediaSession`, no notification, no background playback: the player pauses at `onStop` and is released at lock (Android 17 also requires a foreground service for background audio in apps targeting 37, which GHOST does not declare).
- **Declared:** Media3's sample buffers are Java arrays in the JVM heap, so decrypted video exists there while it plays (L9-M7); `MediaCodec` itself reports metrics to the platform, which `setUsePlatformDiagnostics(false)` does not stop (Media3 documentation; L9-M8). **Corrected by S0 (V-M13, S0-9):** `setUsePlatformDiagnostics(false)` stops ExoPlayer's playback-event reports, but on API 31+ ExoPlayer 1.11.1 still creates a `MediaMetricsManager` playback session and puts its `LogSessionId` in the player's `PlayerId` (`ExoPlayerImpl$Api31.registerMediaMetricsListener` runs unconditionally; only `addAnalyticsListener` depends on the flag), so a platform session exists for every playback and its id is attached to the playback's audio-track record in `dumpsys media.metrics` (API 35), next to the codec records (codec name, resolution, the app's UID and times) that `MediaCodec` writes anyway (API 29 and 35). Transformer with the flag off creates no editing session (`canCollectEditingMetrics` needs it on API 35+). Nothing in Media3's public API turns the playback session off; both are declared in L9-M8. The video preview (§6.2) uses no player and so creates no session.

### 10.3 Inventory of plaintext sinks and their controls

| Sink | Control | Test |
|---|---|---|
| Media3 `SimpleCache`, `CacheDataSource`, `CacheDataSink`, `CacheWriter`, `DownloadManager`, `DownloadService`, `DownloadHelper` (shipped inside `media3-datasource` and `media3-exoplayer`, so a dependency ban cannot exclude them), and `media3-database`'s `StandaloneDatabaseProvider` | G-M1 source ban; **G-M10, the release-dex absence check of the classes R8 removes from a correctly configured app** (S0 V-M14: these, `HttpEngineDataSource`, `AesCipherDataSource`/`Sink`, `DataSourceInputStream`, `InAppFragmentedMp4Muxer`, `EventLogger`, every `media3.database` class and, with G-M3's renderers factory, `DefaultRenderersFactory`, `ImageRenderer`, `BitmapFactoryImageDecoder`, `TextRenderer`, `MetadataRenderer`: a release that keeps one means GHOST code started referencing it) | PM-M10; G-M10 fixture |
| `DefaultDataSource`, `DefaultHttpDataSource`, `HttpEngineDataSource`, `UdpDataSource`, `FileDataSource` (clearnet and file paths `kotlin-clearnet.sh` cannot see in library bytecode) | G-M1; player built only through `GhostMediaDataSource` and GHOST's factories (G-M3's configuration rule). **Always in the release dex** (S0 V-M14): R8 keeps `DefaultHttpDataSource` through the polymorphic `DataSource.Factory.createDataSource()` and `UdpDataSource`/`FileDataSource` as fields of `DefaultDataSource`, so no dex check can back this row; the source rule and the configuration rule do | static; T6 dynamic (Phase 13) |
| Transformer's default muxer and output path | G-M1 (`InAppMp4Muxer`, `Transformer.start` outside `GhostTranscoder`). **Verified in S0 (V-M1, API 29 and 35, 2026-09-25 … 10-04):** 48 exports through `GhostMuxer`, then 24 more per level under the corrected root search, wrote no file: 0 `FileObserver` events and 0 inventory changes over every app directory, the offered path never existed, and a root search of all of `/data` by modification **and** status-change time found only ART's files, the run's own setup and system bookkeeping (package and power statistics; on API 29, whose search had to select by `-cmin`, also files the boot had touched minutes earlier). `InAppMp4Muxer` stays in the release dex (R8 keeps it through `DefaultMuxer` and `Transformer.setUpMetricsCollection`) | sandbox scan |
| Temp and cache files (`createTempFile`, `cacheDir`, `codeCacheDir`, `openFileOutput`, external dirs, `getExternalMediaDirs`) | G-M1; the only file writer is `CiphertextFiles` | sandbox scan |
| `MediaStore` inserts, `MediaScannerConnection` (an insert records GHOST as `OWNER_PACKAGE_NAME`) | G-M1 | `MediaStore` query on the emulator |
| Share intents, `FileProvider`, clipboard, drag and drop | G-M1; T24 (Phase 13) | static |
| Notifications with pictures (`BigPictureStyle`, `setLargeIcon`) | G-M1; T11 | static |
| Thumbnails and previews | RAM cache only (§10.5) | sandbox scan, PM-M9 |
| Platform thumbnailers and in-process decoders (`ContentResolver.loadThumbnail`, `ThumbnailUtils`, `BitmapRegionDecoder`, `ImageView.setImageURI`, `Drawable.createFromStream`/`createFromPath`, `Icon.createWithContentUri`, `AnimatedImageDrawable`, `Movie`): each decodes in the main process or asks a provider to make and cache a thumbnail | G-M3 (source and bytecode rules, §16.1) | static; the decoder spy (T46) |
| Freed RAM the app does not own (JVM arrays after GC, GPU textures, compositor and codec buffers) | none from the app beyond releasing early; GHOST's own bitmaps are `eraseColor(0)`ed before `recycle()` | declared (L9-M7) |
| Logs, crash text | Media3 logging off; T3 canaries | logcat marker scan (F9 §22.32) |
| Backup, device transfer | `allowBackup="false"`, data extraction rules exclude everything; the store is under `noBackupFilesDir` | T4 on the emulator |
| Isolated process memory | one process per request, dead after the reply (§9.2) | T46 PID test; PM-M21 |
| **Task snapshots (OS-written)**: Android persists a Recents snapshot of the last frame as a `.jpg` under `/data/system_ce/<user>/snapshots` (AOSP `TaskSnapshotController.SNAPSHOTS_DIRNAME = "snapshots"`, `BaseAppSnapshotPersister.BITMAP_EXTENSION = ".jpg"`) | every window that shows media, including the gate harness's activity, sets FLAG_SECURE and, on API 33+, `setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)` from Phase 11 (T10's rule brought forward for media windows, X-M11, D27). **Measured in S0 (V-M20):** the system persists a snapshot in every case, but with FLAG_SECURE API 29 and API 35 persist only a placeholder (no pattern pixel of 129 600 sampled), and on API 35 `setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)` alone does the same; API 30–32 are measured by the emulator job (or the checklist) before the gate. Both controls stay (defence in depth) | emulator scan of the snapshot directory with `adb root` (§15.8 item 1); PM-M23 |
| **Tombstones, dropbox, bug reports (OS-written)**: a crashed decoder's tombstone holds its registers, stack and memory near them, so plaintext fragments of the decoded image; `/data/tombstones` and dropbox entries are included in an `adb bugreport` taken on the unlocked device, so they are **not** root-only. **Measured in S0 (V-M20, API 29 and 35):** a native crash of a `:mdec` instance writes the memory around its registers into `tombstone_NN` (text, and on API 35 also `.pb`) and into dropbox (`SYSTEM_TOMBSTONE`, compressed); a bug report taken without root carries the tombstones (and on API 35 the dropbox proto) with the plaintext canary in them; tombstones sit in a fixed number of slots and survive reboots (retention on a running phone unmeasured), dropbox copies expire; a Java crash leaves only a stack | the process per request limits a tombstone to one request's bytes; nothing else is possible from the app | declared (L9-M8); the emulator scan records what a forced `:mdec` crash leaves; it **decodes the text form's hex dumps** before searching and also searches `.pb` files and gunzipped dropbox entries, because the hex dump splits the bytes across 16-byte lines and a plain byte search of the text finds nothing (S0: 13–14 hex-dump lines with the canary, 0 raw matches; S0-16) |
| **Storage-backed swap (OS-written)** on devices that write compressed RAM to flash (zram writeback, vendor "RAM extension" features) | none possible from the app | declared, [I] (L9-M8) |

### 10.4 The ciphertext store

- One file per blob, 65 536 B, random 16-byte hex names, in `noBackupFilesDir/m/`, written by `CiphertextFiles` only (a port with a fault-injecting test implementation, §15.3); every write is `force(true)` and the directory is synced before the transaction that references the file.
- After each write, `Files.getFileAttributeView(path, BasicFileAttributeView::class.java).setTimes(FileTime.fromMillis(0), FileTime.fromMillis(0), null)`, so that `mtime` **and `atime`** carry no time. **Corrected by S0 (V-M16, S0-12):** `setLastModified(0)`, which revision 3 prescribed, zeroes `mtime` on ext4 (API 29 and 35) but leaves `atime` at the creation second, and `/data` is mounted `noatime` there, so it would never move again; `setTimes(0, 0)` zeroes both. `atime` stays 0 only on a `noatime` mount: on a `relatime` mount the first read after the write would stamp it with the viewing time (an `atime` of 0 is older than `mtime`, `ctime` and a day), so S0b records the reference phones' `/data` mount options, and on a `relatime` phone GHOST opens fragment files with `O_NOATIME` (allowed to the file's owner; S1 checks `Os.open` exposes it) or declares the first read time. A crash between the write and `setTimes` leaves a file with real times: `setTimes` is a `CiphertextFiles` fault point (§15.3), and the reconcile re-applies it to every file it keeps. What an app cannot set is declared (L9-M6, Q18): `ctime` (the write and the time-setting call itself stamp it; the inode field holds nanoseconds, set at the kernel's timestamp granularity, 4 ms and 10 ms ticks on the S0 emulators, so consecutive files of one object are 8–12 ms apart and their order and pace are readable); the birth time (`crtime`) on ext4 and on f2fs with `inode_crtime` (unmeasured: S0b); and inode numbers, which the file system allocates close together (S0: 17 files → inodes 352521 … 352538), so one object's 17 … 805 files and the order of objects can be grouped. A preallocated arena file with fixed slots would hide these, at the cost of a slot allocator and in-place overwrites; it is not in Phase 11 (Q18).
- **Reconcile** (media actor): list the directory; delete every file that no `media_blob` row names and that is not in `preparing`; sync the directory. The order matters for a sealer on another thread: the sealer reserves its name before it creates the file, the reconcile lists first, takes the `preparing` snapshot second and reads the rows third (§22.5 row 25). It runs after every transaction that deletes media rows (SWEEP through `MediaPort`, view-once close, `incomplete`, `corrupt`, pool eviction and GC, view-once sender cleanup) and at process start. A crash between a deletion's commit and the reconcile leaves ciphertext whose key is already erased; the next reconcile removes it.
- **Why files, not SQLCipher BLOBs (Q18).** Media does pass through the database already: every received blob is written to `inbox_blob.ciphertext` when Phase 7 fetches it and set to NULL when `MEDIA-INGEST` consumes it (`Schema.kt:370-386`, `InboxStore.kt:392-396`), and the upload window freezes its payloads in `outbox_op` (revision 2 did not say so). What the files avoid is keeping **whole live objects** there: at most `FETCHED_CAP` × 64 KiB = 16 MiB inbound and 4 MiB outbound sit in the database and its WAL at once, instead of up to 51 MiB per object for the object's whole life; the database file never shrinks after deletion (a forensic high-water mark, now bounded by those 20 MiB of media); and video reads would contend with DM transactions on the primary connection (`SupportSqlExecutor` holds it for a whole transaction). The cost of files is inode metadata (L9-M6), declared.

### 10.5 RAM hygiene

Decoded previews live in one `LruCache` of at most 16 MiB, cleared at lock, wipe, `onTrimMemory(TRIM_MEMORY_UI_HIDDEN)` and when an object is deleted; every bitmap GHOST creates from media pixels is overwritten with `eraseColor(0)` before `recycle()` (`recycle` frees pixel memory without any documented overwrite), when its screen closes and when the cache evicts it; native buffers and `SharedMemory` regions are zeroised on release; readers close at lock. Readers ≤ 8, sealers ≤ 2 per context; `ctxClose` closes all of them. **Not claimed:** Media3's sample buffers are Java arrays that the garbage collector frees without zeroising, and GPU textures, the compositor's buffers and the codecs' output buffers belong to the platform; decrypted media may remain in that memory until it is reused (L9-M7; revision 2's "only while shown" overstated this).

---

## 11. Ephemeral media and view-once (normative; ADR-47)

### 11.1 Ephemeral media (P0; ADR-27 point 10)

- **Deletion cascade.** F9's `SWEEP` calls `MediaPort.onMessagesDeleted(tx, ids)` before deleting the messages (`Sweeper.kt:311-314`); the implementation deletes, in that transaction, every `media_object` of those messages with its `key_env`, `media_upload`, `media_fragment` and `media_outbound_op` rows and every `media_blob` no remaining fragment row names (the FK from `media_object.message_id` also cascades from any other deletion of a message, such as a deleted contact; `MediaObjects` deletes the unnamed blobs in that path too — **the sealed ones; the ingested blobs of an object deleted by the foreign key alone stay in the pool until their retention day, §22.5 row 26**), then posts a reconcile after the commit.
- **Checkpoint after every key deletion (RQ-M12; P-M8).** `secure_delete = ON` zeroes freed pages, but an updated or deleted `key_env` survives in two places until a checkpoint: the pre-image page in the main `.db` file (WAL mode writes the new page to the WAL; the checkpoint copies it back over the old one) and the WAL frames. So **every transaction that deletes or nulls a `key_env`** (SWEEP, `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN`, the sender's view-once cleanup, `MEDIA-GC`, the `incomplete` and `corrupt` paths, a contact deletion) is followed by F9 §10.7's checkpoint: `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`, require `busy = 0`, retry 3 times 100 ms apart, else keep `checkpoint_due` and retry at the next sweep (R-K1's checkpoint at every open also applies). `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN` runs it before returning the handle (§11.2). What remains below the file system (flash blocks of the truncated WAL) is outside the app's reach, as for every F9 deletion.
- **Sender.** The sender's copy (files and key) lives as long as its message row, whose countdown starts when the upload ends (D26, §6.3): at `MEDIA-READY`, or at the op deadline for an object that never became ready. A message therefore never expires mid-upload. If the **user** deletes the message or the contact during an upload, the rows and key go at once, the feeder stops, unreleased ops run to their outcome or deadline with ciphertext nothing on the device opens (F9 §21.10: no `Outbox.cancel`; their outcomes are released and ignored), and the descriptor is never sent: a short burst, declared in `LM`.
- **Recipient.** The object goes with the message: at `expire_at_minute` after display, or at the unread deadline `min(E, consume + bucket + 1 h)` (F9 §10.3). An object still `awaiting` or `fetching` goes the same way; blobs arriving later are pooled and dropped at their retention day.
- **Relays.** Honest relays drop fragments and manifest by `deadline_hour + bucket + 1 h` (T30), fragments no later than the descriptor (R-MT3). Every media store receipt passes F9 §10.2 point 6's upper bound, so a relay promising to keep a blob more than σ = 3 d longer than its bucket is detected (and one within σ is tolerated, L7-D27, which applies unchanged). A dishonest relay may keep ciphertext anyway; without `K_f`, erased on both devices, it opens nothing (ADR-27 residues). The relay never learns the timer, only the bucket (T25).
- **T25 bounds for media** (the oracle of §15.5): no `media_object`, `media_upload`, `media_fragment`, `media_outbound_op` row, no `key_env` bytes in the raw database and WAL, and no file of the object after `delete_at_minute + lag + reconcile lag` (unless another live object names the same blob hash, an attack case, P-M8; for an object deleted by a foreign key alone, its ingested blobs wait in the pool up to eight days with no key, §22.5 row 26; and the file system keeps the times of the freed inodes, §22.5 row 27); no fragment bytes in the raw database after the later of that bound and the `deadline_hour` of the object's Phase 7 ops (frozen outbox payloads legitimately live until their op's last possible store, F9 §10.3; on the recipient, a fetched blob's `inbox_blob.ciphertext` is set to NULL at ingest, and the sweep's checkpoint removes the frames that held it); no preview in the RAM cache after the message left the screen; and, on the sender, no `sync_namespace` row or credential of `M_c` after the later of the last op's deadline and the descriptor's final outcome, plus the sweep lag (§5.1, T33). The sender's bound counts from the end of the upload (D26), and the sender's caption and ciphertext may live up to `deadline_hour + timer` from the commit (L9-M13).

### 11.2 View-once (P1; ADR-27 point 12)

- **Sender.** `viewOnce = true` seals with `flags.view_once`, **no preview**, the conversation's TTL and class like any other object (NI-M4). The sender's UI shows a placeholder; once the descriptor has a final outcome other than FAILED (SENT, DEGRADED, or INDETERMINATE after its resends) the sender deletes its files and `key_env` and checkpoints (§6.4, §11.1). A FAILED descriptor keeps them until the deadline for a retry, then the same cleanup runs.
- **Recipient, `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN`** (one transaction on the DM actor): require `state = 'verified'` and `key_env` present; call `readerOpen`, which moves `K_f` into a native reader; set `key_env = NULL`, `state = 'viewing'`; commit; run the TRUNCATE checkpoint of §11.1 (busy = 0, three retries); **only then** return the handle to the UI. If the transaction fails, the reader is closed (zeroised) before the exception propagates. If the checkpoint still reports busy after its retries, the handle is returned anyway (the key is already gone from the logical database, and refusing would lose the object) with `checkpoint_due` set: the old page survives until the next successful checkpoint, a lag declared in L9-M10 and measured by SCN-MD.
- **`VIEW-ONCE-CLOSE`** (when the UI closes the handle, at lock, at wipe, and at process start for any `viewing` row): close the reader, set `state = 'viewed'`, delete the fragment rows and every blob no row names any more, reconcile. The message row stays ("viewed") until its timer.
- **Crash semantics (CS-M5).** A crash before the commit leaves the key in the database and nothing shown: the user can open it again. A crash after the commit loses the object (the key existed only in native memory): at most one display per device, never two. Q11 records the choice (the alternative, erasing after rendering, allows a second view after a crash).
- **Honest limits (never a stronger UI claim).** FLAG_SECURE (T10) blacks out screenshots and recordings; it does not stop a second device or a camera, a modified client that ignores the flag, or a rooted phone (ADR-27 residues). Relay copies live until their TTL (view-once does not shorten it). Before it is opened, a seized device holds the key (wrapped) and the ciphertext. **The sender's side:** the sender's device holds the key and files while the upload runs and, after a FAILED descriptor, until the deadline (§6.4), and the original stays wherever the user picked it from (the gallery), which GHOST neither reads again nor deletes. **Before the view:** `MEDIA-VERIFY` decrypts the object chunk by chunk in native memory to validate it (X-M14). **After the view:** decrypted pixels may survive in memory GHOST does not own (freed JVM arrays, GPU and compositor buffers) until reused (L9-M7). View-once protects honest devices and accidental re-viewing, not against the recipient. It is not forwardable.

---

## 12. Traffic, metadata and residues (ADR-09, ADR-15, ADR-20)

### 12.1 When media traffic happens (normative)

| Traffic | STANDARD | HIGH | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| Own `M_0` listings | Phase 7 schedule (T = 30 s × U[0.5, 1.5] per pair; background W = 90 s): one more listened pair per relay of the union set, about doubling the owner's own list traffic (§5.2) | same | nothing media-related (T19) |
| `get`s on `M_0` | at `M_0`'s list events, within the media fetch budget | same | others' stores into `M_0` (declared, LIMITE L2.1 #3) |
| Stores into `M_c` (manifest, fragments) | when due, in the media store budget after DM work | at `M_c`'s pair events after U[0, 10 min] | the user's send (`MEDIA-COMMIT`, `MEDIA-FEED`); never the conversation's timer (D26) |
| Media identical resends | when the INDETERMINATE outcome is released, within `resendNotAfter` | at pair events | the user's own send |
| Descriptor deposit into `X_c` | right after `MEDIA-READY` | pair event after U[0, 10 min] | the user's send, after its upload |
| Own redeem and `register` for `M_0` | with `X_0` and `P_0`: at activation and restore, and weekly at the EXPIRING PRF time (where a device activated before Phase 11 first adds `M_0`; no step runs at the update, §5.2) | same | the week |
| Anything caused by a descriptor, a download, a validation, a display, a view-once opening, the pool's occupancy or free space | **nothing** (T47) | nothing | — |

### 12.2 Per-event observation matrix (TM §6 rows; Romanian text in Appendix B.3)

| Event | Relay (AD-2) | AD-1 adds | AD-4 / AD-5 | AD-7 (holds my card) | AD-8 (seized device) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I send media to c | `F + 1` × `store(M_c, hash, 65 536, ttl(bucket), minute, the shared media-deposit scope of M_c at that relay, my circuit)` within minutes (fewer, and no descriptor, after a FAILED outcome, the op deadline or my deleting the message); then a 4 KiB deposit into `X_c` | the same on every relay of `set(M_c, w)`; my session ties the `M_c` burst to the `X_c` deposit (L9-M2) | upload `3 × (F + 1) × 64 KiB` | — | the object's ciphertext files and rows until the message expires; the frozen outbox window; outside GHOST, what the system picker kept about the pick (thumbnails, a placeholder or, on API 29, GHOST's last-pick record and a pick counter; S0 V-M21, L9-M15) |
| I receive media | `list(M_0)` at T19 times with my read scope; `F + 1` `get`s at list events | presence; `M_0` and `X_0` listed by one client (L7-D2) | download `(F + 1) × 64 KiB` | with its own token for `M_0`: my media volume and times, never which contact sent what | fetched blobs in `inbox_blob` until ingested; ciphertext files and rows; after expiry, inbox tombstones of `M_0` (F7 retention) |
| I open, view or view once | **nothing** | nothing | nothing | nothing | `viewed` state |
| Media expires | nothing | — | — | — | nothing of the object's rows, key or file contents after the lags (the freed inodes keep their write and deletion times until reused, L9-M6, §22.5 row 27); tombstones remain (L9-M6) |
| A card holder floods `M_0` | stores under the shared deposit scope until each relay's allowance is spent, and under its own write capabilities as far as its tokens go (L7-D16) | — | my forced download, bounded as in §5.3 | — | pooled ciphertext ≤ 512 MiB (oldest evicted) for ≤ 8 d |

### 12.3 Consequences for existing invariants

- **T1:** no new field or value; `M` events are `store`, `get`, `list`, `check`, `redeem`, `register` as today. `privacy-capture.sh` gains a fixture of a media burst (only `size_bucket = 65536` and existing TTLs).
- **T9:** made executable for media (T42).
- **T19:** unchanged; re-tested with media activity (§5.5).
- **T21:** `M_c` and `M_0` calls use their own namespace's circuit; a media upload and a descriptor deposit never share a circuit.
- **T27, T28, T31:** extended to `M` (T47).
- **T2 (Phase 8):** `M_0` adds one owner namespace per client, uniformly (§5.2).

### 12.4 Local forensic residue (AD-8; T33 extended)

| Item | Where | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|
| `media_object` (kind, class, flags, state, destination, manifest hash, wrapped key), `media_fragment` (hashes, ordinals, upload states), `media_blob` (hashes, file names, `retain_until_day`) and `media_outbound_op` (op ids, resend counts) | SQLCipher | with the message; pooled blobs ≤ 8 d |
| ciphertext files: 64 KiB each, random names, `mtime` = `atime` = 0 (on a `noatime` mount, S0-12), **`ctime` in a nanosecond field at the kernel's tick (4–10 ms on the S0 emulators), the birth time where the file system keeps one, and inode numbers that group an object's files** | `noBackupFilesDir/m/` | with the object; orphans until the next reconcile |
| what the system picker keeps about a pick (S0 V-M21, L9-M15): thumbnails of the pictures shown (shared storage and MediaProvider's cache), a zero-filled placeholder named after the picked item (API 35), GHOST's package name with the folder and time of its last pick and a per-file pick counter (API 29's DocumentsUI) | MediaProvider, DocumentsUI, shared storage | outside GHOST's control; they survive reboots, retention on a running phone unmeasured |
| frozen media payloads in `outbox_op` | SQLCipher | until each op's last possible store (F7) |
| fetched, not yet ingested `M_0` blobs (`inbox_blob.ciphertext`, at most 256 × 64 KiB) | SQLCipher and its WAL | until `MEDIA-INGEST` consumes them (seconds while the app runs); their WAL frames until the next checkpoint |
| the sender's `M_c` registration and credentials | `sync_namespace` and the credential tables | until the last op of c's last upload is released and the next sweep (§5.1); revision 2 kept it for the contact's lifetime |
| inbox tombstones of `M_0` (namespace, hash, day) | SQLCipher | `ceil7(day(E) + 24)` (F7 retention, ADR-20 point 6) |
| captions | `dm_message.body_env` | with the message |
| no time column other than `*_day` and `*_hour` with `typeof` CHECKs (T33) | | |

### 12.5 New LIMITE section L9 (media residues; Romanian text in Appendix B.4)

| ID | Residue | Why it remains | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| L9-M1 | Media volume is a strong correlation signal: an upload of `3 × (F + 1) × 64 KiB` and a matching download of `(F + 1) × 64 KiB` are linkable by a network-level adversary (flow correlation such as DeepCorr needs about 900 packets) | no cover traffic (P2) | HIGH delay; classes; cover traffic P2; no UI claim |
| L9-M2 | Relays see the class `F` of every object (11 classes; every image in class 0), the burst into a recipient's `M` and, from the sender's session, the descriptor deposit into the same owner's `X` shortly after | fragments must be stored; the descriptor waits for the upload | classes (D5); one TTL per conversation; **not** the HIGH delay, which stays inside the session (only a descriptor sent in a later session would break the link; Phase 12's decoupling, L7-D2) |
| L9-M3 | Automatic download: every object deposited into my `M` is downloaded (data cost), including from blocked contacts and spammers; turning downloads off is visible to relays as `M` listing stopping | the only reflex-free choice (§7.1); fetching may not depend on local state | allowance; card rotation (Phase 13); Q4 |
| L9-M4 | A card holder can exhaust my weekly media allowance on every relay of my union set (distinct blobs per relay: about 256 MiB per relay of the union set per week) **and** store more under write capabilities bought with its own tokens (L7-D16), all of which I download; it can fill my pool to 512 MiB for 8 days, and under a flood a late descriptor of an honest contact can find its fragments evicted (`incomplete`) | no sender-bought capabilities for honest senders (T27); write grants read and write at relays; fetching may not depend on the pool (T47) | allowance per registration; `POOL_CAP` by eviction; the owner-bound write and read rule of Phase 12 (F9 Q36); card rotation (Phase 13); Q20 |
| L9-M5 | Large media in 5-minute and 1-hour conversations may miss the deadline in background jobs; an object whose fragments expired before the recipient fetched them is `incomplete`; transfers are slow (per-RPC clients) | TTL = conversation bucket (ADR-27 point 11); Phase 7 client per call | foreground transfers; Phase 12 pooling (F-M2) |
| L9-M6 | On a seized device: ciphertext files carry inode `ctime` in a nanosecond field at the kernel's timestamp granularity (stamped by the write and by the time-setting call itself; consecutive files of one object milliseconds apart), a birth time on ext4 and on f2fs with `inode_crtime`, and inode numbers that group an object's 17 … 805 files and order objects, while the object lives **and, in the freed inode, after it (the birth time, the deletion time and a `ctime` and `mtime` stamped by the unlink itself, measured on ext4, §22.5 row 27; the directory's own times at the last write and unlink; f2fs is S0b's); the ingested blobs of an object deleted by a foreign key alone stay in the pool up to eight days (§22.5 row 26)**; `mtime` and `atime` are 0 on a `noatime` mount (on a `relatime` one the first read would set `atime`, S0b); rows show kinds, classes and states; fetched `M_0` blobs wait in `inbox_blob` until ingested; `M_0` tombstones outlive objects by up to ~4 weeks; the OS's per-UID network and battery statistics (LIMITE L1.1) turn each media transfer, a large and distinctive volume, into a timeline at about 2-hour resolution that outlives the object's expiry | Android files; F7 retention; OS statistics | `mtime` = `atime` = 0 (`setTimes`, S0-12); files deleted with the object; ciphertext only; a slot-arena file is the rejected alternative (Q18); cover traffic and fixed-length jobs (Phase 12, P2) |
| L9-M7 | Decrypted video lives in the JVM heap (Media3 sample buffers) while it plays; decoded pixels in native memory while shown; freed JVM arrays, GPU textures and the compositor's and codecs' buffers are not zeroised and may keep plaintext until reused, after display and after a view-once view | Media3 is Java; platform buffers | playback only in the foreground; release at lock; small buffers; `eraseColor(0)` before `recycle()` for GHOST's own bitmaps; native buffers zeroised |
| L9-M8 | `MediaCodec` reports codec metrics to the platform (`dumpsys media.metrics`: codec, resolution, the app's UID, times), and the platform may share diagnostics with Google if the user opted in; **on API 31+ ExoPlayer creates a platform playback session whatever its diagnostics flag, whose id is attached to the playback's audio-track record** (S0 V-M13); a crashed decoder's tombstone and dropbox entry hold the memory around its registers, i.e. plaintext of that request's image (measured in S0, V-M20), and are included in an `adb bugreport` that anyone holding the unlocked device can take (not root-only); tombstones survive reboots in a fixed number of slots; on devices with storage-backed swap, heap pages may reach flash | platform services | ExoPlayer's playback-event reports and Transformer's editing session off; one isolated process per request; FLAG_SECURE against task snapshots |
| L9-M9 | The recipient gets the pixels, sound, duration, the aspect ratio (rounded to 16 px in images; as bars in video), the image's long-edge step, a source frame rate below 30 fps, **the source's rotation** (0°, 90°, 180° or 270°: Media3 keeps it whenever its axis matches the output's, S0; it usually tells front from back camera) and, for a video retried at 15 fps, that its first attempt exceeded its class (§6.2); the camera's sensor noise (PRNU) and the sender's encoder parameters (including whether the phone's encoder supports High profile and which colour matrix it codes, S0-13) survive re-encoding | physics and codecs; frames are dropped, never invented; Media3's rotation handling | canonical geometry, frame-rate cap, audio format and pinned encoder settings (D29, S0-22); Q45 (normalise the rotation); watermarking P2 (LIMITE:138) |
| L9-M10 | View-once does not stop a second device or a camera, a modified client or a rooted phone; relay copies live until their TTL; a crash after opening loses the item; if the checkpoint after opening stays busy, the old `key_env` page survives in the WAL until the next successful checkpoint; the sender's device keeps the key and files while uploading and, after a FAILED descriptor, until the deadline; the original stays in the sender's gallery; the recipient's validation decrypts the object in native memory before the view (X-M14); pixels may survive in memory GHOST does not own (L9-M7) | ADR-27 residues; SQLite WAL; the upload and retry need the copy | FLAG_SECURE (T10); key erased and checkpointed before display; the sender's cleanup at the descriptor's final outcome |
| L9-M11 | Received and picked images are decoded by platform decoders inside an isolated process, one per request: an exploit there sees that request's bytes and can lie about its pixels (for a picked file, shape the JPEG GHOST then sends), but not reach keys, files, the network or any other request; video codecs run in platform processes | platform decoders are the only practical ones | canonical JPEG and fMP4 only; isolation per request (T46); patched devices |
| L9-M12 | Every identity pays one more token per relay per week and lists `M_0` whether or not it uses media: one more listened pair per relay, about doubling its own list traffic (data and battery) | uniformity (D11); T19 | — |
| L9-M13 | A sender's media burst can end short of `F + 1` stores, with no descriptor after it, after a FAILED outcome, at the op deadline or when the sender's user deletes the message or contact; the sender's caption and ciphertext live until the upload ends plus the timer, at most `deadline_hour + timer` from the commit (≈ 1 h 5 min for a 5-minute timer) | the countdown must not cut bursts inside a bucket (D26); Phase 7 has no cancel | the deadline depends on the bucket only; no truncation by the timer |
| L9-M14 | Fan-out: one file sent to several contacts is several objects of one class, uploaded as bursts into several `M` namespaces from the same session, which links those recipients to one sender and to each other under AD-1 | session co-occurrence and one circuit per session's deposits (L7-D2, L7-D5) | none in Phase 11 (a fresh key per destination hides only the bytes, P-M5); Phase 12's decoupling |
| L9-M15 | What the OS records about a pick (measured in S0, V-M21): **API 35 photo picker:** a 600-px JPEG thumbnail of the picture in MediaProvider's private Glide cache and another in shared storage (`Pictures/.thumbnails/<id>.jpg`, MediaProvider's, mode 0660), a zero-filled placeholder named after the picked item and written at the pick (`.transforms/synthetic/picker/…/<id>.jpg`), the picker's MediaStore mirror (`picker.db`), GHOST's task record; no grant record; the picture is handed over with its GPS fields zeroed. **API 29 DocumentsUI:** GHOST's package name with the folder and time of its last pick (`lastAccess.db`), a per-file pick counter keyed by `String.hashCode()` of the document URI (`pickCountRecord.db`), and a 600-px JPEG thumbnail of **every picture the picker displays** in shared storage (`Pictures/.thumbnails/<id>.jpg`, mode 0664, readable by path by apps with the storage permission and legacy storage access); the file is handed over unchanged. Usage-stats entries for the picker activity on both. These survive reboots; their retention on a running phone is unmeasured (S0b), as are API 30–34 and 36–37 and the Play services picker that `PickVisualMedia` may open on an API 29–30 phone with Play services | the OS's picker, not GHOST | the photo picker on API 30+; STRIP removes what the stream carries; declared per API level; Q42 |

---

## 13. Modules, JNI surface, schema, threads (normative)

### 13.1 Module layout

| Module / package | Contents | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `client-core/media` = `ghost-client-media` (Rust, new) | constants and classes (§3.1, §3.6), derivations, the sealer and reader state machines, STREAM, the manifest and descriptor codecs, the magic-byte sniffer, the JPEG canonicaliser and the fMP4 walker with the `mfra` builder; no JNI, no I/O, no clock, no logging, no state | `chacha20poly1305`, `hkdf`, `hmac`, `sha2`, `zeroize` (already pinned) |
| `client-core/dm` (`ghost-client-dm`) | gains the dependency on `ghost-client-media`, the `MediaNative` JNI class behind `jni-bridge` with sealer and reader registries inside the DM context, the `M` labels, the `MediaFileKey` wrap name, and the key-slot handling in `encrypt`/`decrypt`/`sessionInbound`/`l0` | as today + `ghost-client-media` |
| `:crypto-bridge` | `MediaNative` (the closed list of §13.2), `MediaResult`, the typed facade `MediaCrypto` | as today |
| `:media` `org.ghost.media` (new code in the existing module) | `.store` (`MediaObjects`, the tables, `CiphertextFiles`, reconcile), `.send` (`MediaPreparer`, `GhostTranscoder`, `GhostMuxer`, `SealingChannel`, `MediaFeeder`), `.receive` (`MediaIngest`, `MediaBinder`, `MediaVerifier`), `.play` (`GhostMediaDataSource`, player factory, preview cache), `.sandbox` (`MediaDecodeService`, the only package that may reference platform decoders), `.api` (`MediaDestination`, `MediaHandle`, states) | `:crypto-bridge`, `:sync` API, `:storage`, Media3 |
| `:messaging` | kind 5 in `SEND`/`RECV`/`OUTCOME` (the object's TTL, the request rule), the `MediaPort` implementation, `sendMedia`/`openMedia`/`openViewOnce`/`retryMedia`/`setConversationOnScreen`/`mediaPreview`, `M_0` in the owner steps, the `M_c` orphan rule; **its unit tests host `MediaWorld`** (§15.1) | `:media` |
| `:sync` | the media lane (§5.5) | — |
| `:storage` | migration M (§13.6) | — |
| `:app` | wiring, the isolated service in the manifest, the `Process.isIsolated()` early return in `GhostApp.onCreate` (§9.2), Media3 logging off | all |

**Why the cryptography is in Rust, by evidence (D8).**

| Criterion | Rust (`ghost-client-media` via `MediaNative`) | Kotlin (JCA/Conscrypt ChaCha20-Poly1305, BouncyCastle HKDF) |
|---|---|---|
| ADR-19 ("nicio logică de protocol în Kotlin"), ADR-20 point 8 | complies | a deviation to record |
| File key off the JVM heap (D7, P-M8) | yes: `K_f` lives in native memory and `key_env` | no: the key sits in GC-managed arrays that cannot be zeroised |
| One implementation for DMs and channels | the Phase 10 MLS crate links the same crate | a second implementation in Rust for MLS, or JNI back into Kotlin |
| New shipped dependencies | no new external crate (every primitive already pinned); the workspace crate itself needs its allowlist line (G-M7) | none |
| Test infrastructure | the existing host build runs the real code on the JVM | JVM-native |
| Performance | RustCrypto `chacha20` has a NEON backend, `poly1305` 0.9.1 only soft and AVX2 (lock and registry sources). **Measured in S0 (V-M9) on the x86_64 emulators (API 29 and 35):** seal 155–343 MB/s (keeping or streaming the fragments, hashing included), open 227–340 MB/s, a 50 MiB object streamed in ≈ 1.4 MB of memory; arm64 is S0b | Conscrypt uses BoringSSL assembly; **measured in the app on the same emulators: 96–156 MB/s for encryption alone** (no hashing), so the Rust path is not slower there (revision 3 said Conscrypt "is faster"; S0 review I19) |
| Verdict | **chosen**: at Tor speeds (a few Mbit/s) throughput is irrelevant; custody, ADR-19 and a single implementation decide | rejected |

Rules: `:media` references no `org.ghost.network`, `java.net`, `javax.net`, socket or HTTP class and no coroutines (G-DM1's rule extended); no `catch (Exception)` (typed catches, `sync-no-catch-all.sh`); no logging (T3).

### 13.2 `MediaNative`: the closed JNI surface (gate G-R6 gains a second list)

All functions are `static native` on `org.ghost.crypto.bridge.MediaNative`, take the DM context handle `h` (F9 §2.5), byte arrays, direct `ByteBuffer`s and ints, return `code(1) ‖ TLV fields`, perform no I/O, read no clock, log nothing, and catch panics under the silent hook (F9 §2.6). Sealers and readers are native registry entries owned by the context; `ctxClose` zeroises all of them.

| # | Function | Main outputs | Codes besides `OK` |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | `sealBegin(h, destId, kind, viewOnce, maxClass)` | `sealerId`, `object_id` | `LIMIT` (2 sealers), `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE` |
| M2 | `sealAppend(h, sealerId, plaintext direct buffer, len, out direct buffer)` | the fragments of every chunk completed by this call and their hashes | `NON_CANONICAL` (walker), `OVERSIZE` (> `MAX_CONTENT` or above `maxClass`, R-MT2), `INDEX_REUSED` (bug) |
| M3 | `sealFinish(h, sealerId, preview)` | the padding chunks' fragments and hashes, the manifest blob, `manifest_hash`, `class_code`, `content_len`, the walked dimensions (for video the displayed geometry: the coded 1 280 × 720 turned by the `tkhd` matrix, S0-20) and duration, `key_env` bound to `(out, destId, object_id, manifest_hash)` | `NON_CANONICAL` (walker incomplete, preview not canonical or outside the preview geometry of D29), `OVERSIZE` |
| M4 | `sealAbort(h, sealerId)` | — | — |
| M5 | `readerOpen(h, keyEnv, role, destId, objectId, manifestBlob, descriptorFields)` | `readerId`, the manifest fields (kind, flags, class, `content_len`, dimensions, duration, preview, hashes) | `NOT_OPEN`, `COMMIT_MISMATCH`, `HASH_MISMATCH`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `STATE_CORRUPT` (`key_env` does not open under its binding), `LIMIT` (8 readers) |
| M6 | `readerChunk(h, readerId, index, fragments direct buffer, out direct buffer, mode ∈ {validate, read})` | the chunk's content bytes (read) or nothing (validate) | `NOT_OPEN` (tag), `NON_CANONICAL` (pad, walker), `HASH_MISMATCH`, `INDEX_RANGE` |
| M7 | `readerValidateFinish(h, readerId)` | — | `NON_CANONICAL` (walker incomplete, chunks missing, manifest dimensions or duration unlike the walked stream) |
| M8 | `readerClose(h, readerId)` | — | — |
| M9 | `jpegCanonicalize(bytes)` | canonical bytes | `NON_CANONICAL` |
| M10 | `sniff(first64)` | a source type from the allowlist of §6.1: JPEG, PNG, static WebP, or an ISO-BMFF video whose `ftyp` brands are not HEIF/AVIF image brands (S0-4, S0-21) | `UNSUPPORTED` |

`ghost-dm-host` adds two test-only symbols, `MediaNative.testVectors` and `MediaNative.testSealBeginWithKey` (a sealer whose `K_f` and `object_id` come from the world's seeded generator, so the model relay can re-derive media blobs instead of storing them, §15.6), and G-R6 checks that no shipped library exports either (the `DmEquivalence` precedent). `ErrorPolicyTest`'s 21 network categories are unchanged; `MediaResultCodesTest` is exhaustive over the new enumeration.

### 13.3 `:sync` additions (normative)

- `TrafficPolicy` gains `mediaStoresPerPass = 16`, `mediaStoresPerPairEvent = 8`, `mediaForegroundFetchesPerEvent = 64`, `mediaBackgroundFetchesPerEvent = 128`, `mediaFetchesPerItem = 8`, `mediaBackgroundRelayWorkMillis = 45 000` (validated like the existing fields; the last must be below `backgroundRelayWorkMillis`).
- `StoreStep` orders due ops by consumer priority first and takes media stores only after the pass's non-media stores (§5.5); `FetchStep` reads the pair's consumer to pick the budget, fetches a media bundle in slices of `mediaFetchesPerItem` and reposts the remainder behind queued non-media items; the work budget charges media calls to a separate per-relay share.
- No new API: `Inbox.want` is **not** added (X-M6); `Consumer.MEDIA`, `CapabilitySource.LOCAL` and the registration API already exist.
- `sync-exit-gate`'s worlds gain a media consumer with bursts of 17 and 129 blobs; the report line is unchanged apart from the scenario list; the 17 Phase 7 mutants stay detected.

### 13.4 `:messaging` and `:entitlement` changes

- `SEND`: a kind-5 item is sendable only when its object is `uploaded`; `encrypt` receives `mediaKeyEnv`; the item's TTL and deadline come from `media_upload` (R-MT4), not from `ttlFor(conv, kind)`. `RECV` and the held replay: a kind-5 L0, from `decrypt` or `sessionInbound`, calls `MediaObjects.onDescriptor` with its `key_env` in the same transaction, **except in a request**, where the `key_env` is dropped and no object is created (D28). `OUTCOME`: a view-once descriptor's final outcome other than FAILED triggers the sender cleanup (§6.4); a descriptor's final outcome may complete the `M_c` orphan condition (§5.1). `SWEEP`: the `MediaPort` implementation (§11.1), then the checkpoint; the `M_c` orphan rows go through F9 §21.12. The sender countdown of a kind-5 row is set by `MEDIA-READY` or the deadline (D26).
- Owner steps (`ACTIVATE`, `OWNER-WEEK`, `KEEPER`, `REGROWS`) include `M_0`; `OWNER-WEEK` adds a missing `M_0` at the EXPIRING PRF time; there is no one-time upgrade step (§5.2).
- `:entitlement` needs no code change: `M_0` is one more `REDEEM` namespace with pending registrations (F9 §13.4); the JD8 worlds count three owner namespaces.

### 13.5 Transaction inventory (additions to F9 §7.2)

| Tx | Actor | Trigger | Writes | After commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MEDIA-COMMIT` | DM | preparation finished (a user send) | `media_object`, `media_blob` (sealed), `media_fragment` (bound, `unsent`), `media_upload`, `dm_message` (expiry upper bound), `dm_outbound_item`, `M_c` registration and credentials | remove from `preparing`; post `MEDIA-FEED` |
| `MEDIA-FEED` | DM | commit; `OUTCOMES` for MEDIA; retry; time-triggered pass | `Outbox.enqueue` (MEDIA), `media_outbound_op`, ordinals `open` | `requestExpedite()` in STANDARD |
| `MEDIA-OUTCOME` | DM | `OUTCOMES` for MEDIA | `Outbox.release`, ordinal states, resend ops, object `failed`; when c's uploads are all over, c's `media_upload` rows deleted and `dm_orphan_namespace(M_c)` inserted (§5.1) | `MEDIA-READY` check |
| `MEDIA-READY` | DM | last final outcome | object `uploaded`; `expire_at_minute` lowered (countdown starts); the item becomes sendable; F9 `SEND` for it in the same pass | as `SEND` |
| `MEDIA-DEADLINE` | DM | time-triggered pass after `deadline_hour` | object `failed`; `expire_at_minute` lowered; the `M_c` orphan rule (§5.1) | — |
| `MEDIA-INGEST` | media | `INBOX` hint for MEDIA; consumer tail | `markConsumed`, `media_blob` (ingested), `expected` rows of every object naming the hash → `bound`, pool eviction | `MEDIA-BIND` / completeness check; reconcile after an eviction |
| `MEDIA-BIND` | media | a manifest or an awaiting object meets its pair | `media_fragment` (bound, expected), object `fetching` / `corrupt` | — |
| `MEDIA-VERIFY` | media | object `complete`; a read failure after `verified` | object `verified` / `corrupt`; a `corrupt` object's references released | listener hint; reconcile |
| `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN` / `-CLOSE` | DM | UI; lock; wipe; start | `key_env = NULL`, `viewing` / `viewed`, fragment rows and unnamed blobs deleted | checkpoint (OPEN, before the handle); reconcile |
| `MEDIA-GC` | media | time-triggered pass | pooled blobs past their day; `incomplete` objects' references | checkpoint if a `key_env` went; reconcile |
| F9 `RECV`, held replay, `SWEEP`, `OUTCOME` | DM | as F9 | also `media_object` via `MediaObjects` / `MediaPort` (none for a request, D28); the view-once sender cleanup; the `M_c` orphan rule | checkpoint and reconcile after `SWEEP` and after the cleanup |

No media transaction performs network I/O. File writes happen before the transaction that references them; file deletions after the commit that dereferences them (reconcile).

### 13.6 Schema migration M (normative; one transaction, append-only, fail-closed; the F9 §13.6 practice)

**Number.** The next free version at merge. `Schema.CURRENT_VERSION` is 5 today (`ghost/android/storage/src/main/kotlin/org/ghost/storage/Schema.kt:120`), but two other schema changes are pending and may land first: **Phase 9's open defects** in LIMITE L5 each need a column and a migration (the TIMER_SET bucket on the pending path needs a column in `dm_outbound_item`; the FAILED-control reschedule needs an attempts column in `dm_scheduled`; F9 §22.21 item 2, §22.13 item 9), and **Phase 10's migrations 6 and 7** (its proposed design, branch `p10-design`, `faza10-forum.md` §9; its migration 7 drops the v1 channel tables and leaves `media` and `media_blobs` to this phase). Rules: (a) migration M's rebuilds copy the columns of the tables **as they are at merge**, generated from the current shared definitions, never from a copy of v5 frozen in this design; (b) if the owner decides the Phase 9 fixes before S3, their columns are added **in M's rebuild** of `dm_outbound_item` (one rebuild instead of two, Q27); (c) **numbers are reserved now, not at merge (Q38):** Phase 10 keeps 6 and 7, so M is 8, or the next number after a Phase 9 fix migration that lands first; the ADR and invariant ranges are reserved the same way (ADR-44 … ADR-47, T42–T47, R-M8).

1. **Guard and drop the v1 media tables.** `media` and `media_blobs` (`Schema.kt:211-225`) never had a writer: a `vM_migration_guard (row_count … CHECK (row_count = 0))` counts both; then `DROP TABLE media_blobs; DROP TABLE media`. `expectedTables` loses them.
2. **Rebuild `dm_message`** (`Schema.kt:972-1001`) with the explicit-column copy of migration 4's outbox rebuild (G-9, E-5): only `kind IN (1, 2)` becomes `kind IN (1, 2, 5)`, the column CHECK on `body_env` becomes `length(body_env) BETWEEN 28 AND 61485`, and `(kind = 1) = (body_env IS NOT NULL)` becomes `(kind IN (1, 5)) = (body_env IS NOT NULL)` with two table CHECKs, `kind <> 1 OR length(body_env) >= 30` (today's text minimum, unchanged) and `kind <> 5 OR length(body_env) BETWEEN 28 AND 1052` (the content-key envelope's 28 bytes of overhead, `ContentCipher.OVERHEAD`, plus at most `MAX_CAPTION_BYTES`; an empty caption is a 28-byte envelope); every other column, CHECK, index and trigger (`dm_message_identity`, `dm_message_state`, `dm_message_expiry`, `dm_message_no_replace`) is recreated from shared constants; the `legacy_alter_table = OFF` guard and the `pragma_foreign_key_check` guard of migration 4 are repeated.
3. **Rebuild `dm_outbound_item`** (`Schema.kt:1004-1016`) the same way: `kind BETWEEN 1 AND 4` becomes `BETWEEN 1 AND 5`, plus the Phase 9 column if Q27 applies, nothing else. **Corrected by S3 (§22.5, F-S3-1): steps 2 and 3 are one rebuild of five tables.** `DROP TABLE` of a parent first runs an implicit `DELETE`, which fires the `ON DELETE CASCADE` of its children while foreign keys are on — they are on inside the migration's transaction, where `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is a no-op —, so `dm_outbound_op`, `dm_pending_send` and `dm_reaction` are rebuilt with the two tables, unchanged, and the old tables are dropped children first.
4. **Create the media tables:**

```sql
CREATE TABLE media_object (
  object_id      BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(object_id) = 16),
  role           TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('out', 'in')),
  dest_id        BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(dest_id) = 16),       -- the contact (DM); in key_env's binding (§4.2)
  kind           INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN (1, 2)),
  view_once      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (view_once IN (0, 1)),
  class_code     INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (class_code BETWEEN 0 AND 10),
  manifest_hash  BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(manifest_hash) = 32),
  key_env        BLOB CHECK (key_env IS NULL OR length(key_env) = 60),
  state          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('uploading', 'uploaded', 'failed', 'awaiting', 'fetching', 'complete',
                                                'verified', 'corrupt', 'incomplete', 'undecodable', 'viewing', 'viewed')),
  message_id     BLOB REFERENCES dm_message(message_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,        -- NULL only for a test or Phase 10 destination
  decode_failures INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (decode_failures BETWEEN 0 AND 2),
  incomplete_after_hour INTEGER CHECK (incomplete_after_hour IS NULL
                          OR (typeof(incomplete_after_hour) = 'integer' AND incomplete_after_hour % 3600 = 0)),
  CHECK (kind <> 1 OR class_code = 0),                                            -- every image is class 0
  CHECK ((role = 'out') = (state IN ('uploading', 'uploaded', 'failed'))),
  CHECK (state NOT IN ('viewing', 'viewed', 'corrupt', 'incomplete') OR key_env IS NULL),
  CHECK (state NOT IN ('viewing', 'viewed') OR view_once = 1),
  CHECK (role = 'out' OR incomplete_after_hour IS NOT NULL)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_media_object_message ON media_object(message_id) WHERE message_id IS NOT NULL;

CREATE TABLE media_upload (
  object_id     BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL REFERENCES media_object(object_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  namespace_id  BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES sync_namespace(namespace_id),
  ttl_seconds   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (ttl_seconds IN (3600, 86400, 604800, 2592000)),
  deadline_hour INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(deadline_hour) = 'integer' AND deadline_hour % 3600 = 0),
  retry_gen     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (retry_gen BETWEEN 0 AND 3)          -- user retries of the object
) WITHOUT ROWID;

-- One row and one file per distinct ciphertext blob; any number of fragment rows may name it (D25).
CREATE TABLE media_blob (
  blob_hash        BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(blob_hash) = 32),
  file_name        BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(file_name) = 16),
  origin           TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (origin IN ('sealed', 'ingested')),
  retain_until_day INTEGER CHECK (retain_until_day IS NULL
                     OR (typeof(retain_until_day) = 'integer' AND retain_until_day >= 0)),
  CHECK ((origin = 'ingested') = (retain_until_day IS NOT NULL))
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_media_blob_file ON media_blob(file_name);
CREATE INDEX idx_media_blob_retention ON media_blob(retain_until_day) WHERE retain_until_day IS NOT NULL;

CREATE TABLE media_fragment (
  object_id     BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES media_object(object_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  ordinal       INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (ordinal BETWEEN -1 AND 803),                  -- -1 = the manifest
  blob_hash     BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(blob_hash) = 32),                          -- not unique: D25
  state         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('expected', 'bound')),
  upload_state  TEXT CHECK (upload_state IS NULL
                  OR upload_state IN ('unsent', 'open', 'sent', 'degraded', 'indeterminate', 'failed')),
  PRIMARY KEY (object_id, ordinal)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE INDEX idx_media_fragment_hash ON media_fragment(blob_hash);
CREATE INDEX idx_media_fragment_open ON media_fragment(object_id) WHERE upload_state = 'open';

-- Maps a Phase 7 operation back to its blob (the F9 dm_outbound_op pattern, Schema.kt:1017-1027).
CREATE TABLE media_outbound_op (
  operation_id  BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(operation_id) = 16),
  object_id     BLOB NOT NULL,
  ordinal       INTEGER NOT NULL,
  retry_gen     INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (retry_gen BETWEEN 0 AND 3),
  resend_count  INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (resend_count BETWEEN 0 AND 2),
  FOREIGN KEY (object_id, ordinal) REFERENCES media_fragment(object_id, ordinal) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  UNIQUE (object_id, ordinal, retry_gen, resend_count)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
```

5. **Triggers:** `media_object_fixed` (identity, role, destination, kind, view_once, class, manifest hash, message immutable); `media_object_key` (an UPDATE of `key_env` may only set NULL); `media_object_state` (the transitions of §7.6); `media_fragment_state` (`expected → bound` only; `upload_state` only along `unsent → open → {sent, degraded, indeterminate, failed}`, and `failed → unsent` only together with a `retry_gen` increment of its object); `media_fragment_role` (`upload_state` is non-NULL exactly for fragments of `out` objects, and every fragment of an `out` object is `bound`); `media_fragment_bound_blob` (a `bound` row names an existing `media_blob`); `media_upload_monotonic` (`retry_gen` never decreases); `media_outbound_op_fixed` (rows are never updated); REPLACE guards for the five tables (the F9 `replaceGuard` helper). Deleting a `media_blob` that a `media_fragment` row still names is refused by a trigger (`media_blob_referenced`), so "a file goes only when nothing references it" is a schema property, not only a code path. `expectedTables` and `expectedTriggers` gain them; `DmSchemaIntrospectionTest` (T33) gains `incomplete_after_hour`, `deadline_hour` and `retain_until_day`.

`G-DM4` (no `REPLACE`, no upsert) and `G-DM6` (no FTS) cover the media SQL.

### 13.7 Threads and wiring

- **DM actor** (unchanged, F9 §13.7): every transaction that touches `dm_*` rows, including `MEDIA-COMMIT`, `MEDIA-FEED`, `MEDIA-OUTCOME`, `MEDIA-READY`, `VIEW-ONCE-*`.
- **Media actor** (new, `ghost-media`, one thread): `MEDIA-INGEST`, `MEDIA-BIND`, `MEDIA-VERIFY`, `MEDIA-GC`, reconcile, the `preparing` set. It never writes `dm_*` rows. Both actors write media rows only inside transactions, so SQLite serialises them; the only cross-actor state is `preparing`, owned by the media actor.
- **Media worker** (one thread, `ghost-media-prep`): sandbox calls, Transformer, sealing; no transaction.
- **Consumer tail** (F9 §13.3 item 6): drains `Consumer.MEDIA` in background sessions and quiet runs after the transport closed, so session close times cannot depend on inbound media volume (the PM-25 argument).
- **Lock and wipe:** close readers, sealers and the sandbox binding, release players, clear the preview cache and the on-screen signal; wipe also deletes `noBackupFilesDir/m/`. They hang off `GhostApp.onLock`, `onUnlock` and `onWipe`, which have **no caller** before Phase 13's lock screen and wipe flow (`GhostApp.kt:166-185`); in Phase 11 only the gate harness calls them (§15.8).

### 13.8 Keys at rest

`K_f` is stored only as `key_env` (§4.2); `K_p`, `K_m`, `commit` are derived per call in native memory and zeroised; `session_state_key` wraps nothing new beyond `key_env`; no media secret is derived from the seed except `RM_{i,s}` (re-derived, never stored). The content key still wraps captions (`body_env`) and `l0_env` (with a zero key slot).

---

## 14. Invariants (new T42–T47; changes to T3, T4, T5, T9, T10, T20, T21, T25, T27, T28, T31, T33, T35)

### 14.1 New invariants (Romanian rows for INVARIANTS.md in Appendix B.2)

| ID | Invariant | Verification | Executable from | Code |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T42** Uniform media objects | every media blob is exactly 65 536 B with no cleartext header; an object whose upload completes is `F + 1` blobs with `F ∈ CLASSES` = `class(content_len)` (a shorter burst only after FAILED, the op deadline or a user deletion, never because of the timer inside a bucket); images are class 0; a destination change (another recipient, a forward) is a new object: no two honest objects share a blob hash or a 16-byte ciphertext window | vectors; property test over kinds × sizes; byte χ² per offset over 10 000 fragments; JM1–JM3; NI-M3, NI-M7; mutants PM-M1 … PM-M4, PM-M6, PM-M20 | Phase 11 | `client-core/media`, media world |
| **T43** Metadata-zero (T5 made exact) | the decrypted content and preview of every object are canonical baseline JPEG or canonical fMP4 (§8.2, §8.3) produced by a re-encode of every track (never a transmux): no APPn, COM, trailer, `udta`, `meta`, `uuid`, location, device, software or time atom, no source bitstream, no disallowed SEI; the geometry, frame-rate cap and audio format are the canonical ones (D29), not the source's; the sealer refuses and the reader rejects anything else; AAC raw data blocks are declared opaque | the T5 corpus (§15.7) through the real Android pipeline, including sources that already match the output format and sources with distinctive dimensions, frame rates and audio formats: ExifTool finds only an allowlist of structural tags with constant values (X-M13), a canary search finds nothing, the output's SPS/VUI carry GHOST's pinned settings (level 3.1, no B-frames, the colour label of the calibration or no VUI colour description; S0-13, S0-22) and none of the source's values, the colour grid decodes to its colours with that label, orientation goldens hold, the output geometry and audio format are the canonical ones; walker property tests; mutants PM-M11, PM-M12, PM-M18, PM-M26 | Phase 11 (emulator or signed device run) | `android/media` tests, `client-core/media` |
| **T44** No plaintext media at rest | GHOST writes no plaintext media byte, preview or decoded pixel to a file, database, cache, log, clipboard, `MediaStore`, notification or another app, and every window that shows media denies the OS its task snapshot; the only persistent media bytes GHOST writes are relay-identical ciphertext in `noBackupFilesDir/m/` and, bounded and transiently, in Phase 7's SQLCipher tables (`inbox_blob`, `outbox_op`); the OS's tombstones, dropbox entries and storage-backed swap are declared | G-M1 (with its writer allowlist), G-M2, G-M3; G-M10, the release-dex absence check (V-M14, S0-10); the emulator sandbox scan and the system-directory scan (snapshots, tombstones, dropbox; §15.8); the `MediaStore` owner query; mutants PM-M9, PM-M10, PM-M23 | Phase 11 (static: PR; dynamic: emulator) | `android/media`, `scripts/gates/` |
| **T45** Media key custody and erasure | `K_f` exists only in native memory and as `key_env` bound to its role, destination, object and manifest; it is never in the JVM heap, never in `l0_env` or `body_env`, whichever DM function (13 or 14) decrypted its L0; it is deleted with its message (SWEEP) and before the first display of a view-once object, each deletion followed by a WAL checkpoint; it is never stored for a descriptor that arrived in a request (D28); after the lags no row, `key_env` byte (database and WAL) or file of the object remains | key-slot tests through functions 13 and 14 and the held replay (every L0 Kotlin sees has a zero slot); binding tests; the request case; T25 media cases with raw `.db` and `-wal` scans; SCN-MD, SCN-ME; seizure check (§15.6); mutants MC-M6, MC-M7, MC-M19, PM-M13, PM-M17, PM-M22, PM-M24 | Phase 11 | `client-core/dm`, `android/media`, `android/messaging` |
| **T46** Decoder confinement | no received or picked image byte is parsed by a native decoder in the main process; received images reach the sandbox only after canonical validation; each strip or decode request runs in its own isolated process that dies after the reply, and the Application does nothing in it; video is parsed only by Media3's `FragmentedMp4Extractor` after validation and decoded only by `MediaCodec`; nothing is decoded in the background, outside the conversation named on screen, or for requests and blocked contacts | G-M3 (platform decoder and thumbnail APIs only in `org.ghost.media.sandbox`, by a source rule and a bytecode rule over GHOST's classes; every Media3 instance built with GHOST's renderers, extractors and bitmap loader, S0-21) and G-M10 (no default renderer in the release dex); the container-refusal test (§15.8 item 6); a decoder spy in tests with the on-screen signal; the sandbox crash suite; distinct PIDs per request; the emulator binding test; mutants PM-M14, PM-M21, MC-M23 | Phase 11 | `android/media`, `android/app` |
| **T47** Media traffic rules | `M` is relay-blind (T26), deposits into one `M` at one relay share one scope (T27), media blobs and their descriptor use the TTL bucket and deadline fixed at the object's commit (T30, R-MT4), clients read only their own `M` (T31), and no descriptor, download, validation, display, opening, pool occupancy, binding state or free space causes or suppresses traffic (T28); `M_0` is registered and paid by every identity whatever its media activity, only at activation, restore or the EXPIRING PRF time | JM4–JM7; NI-M1, NI-M2, NI-M4, NI-M5, NI-M6; G-DM2 extended (G-M6); mutants PM-M5, PM-M7, PM-M8, PM-M16, PM-M19, PM-M25, PM-M28 | Phase 11 | `android/messaging`, `android/media`, `android/sync`, media world |
