## 15. Tests and the exit-gate argument

### 15.1 Infrastructure

- **Rust.** `ghost-client-media` unit, vector and property tests in the `rust` job; seeded decoder and walker property runs of 10⁶ inputs per CI run (the F9 R-C4 pattern: never panics, never over-reads, `NON_CANONICAL` or `OK` only); the `nonce-registry` feature in every test build (§3.7).
- **JVM.** `MediaWorld` lives in **`:messaging`'s unit tests** (`android/messaging/src/test/kotlin/org/ghost/messaging/media/`), next to F9's `DmWorld`, which it extends. Revision 2 put it in `:media`'s tests, but `:messaging` depends on `:media` (§13.1), and `DmWorld` sits in `:messaging`'s tests with the sync and entitlement harness sources, the friend jars and the forwarding of the `-Dghost.dm.*` harness properties (`messaging/build.gradle.kts:22-49, :80-102, :131-165`): reusing it from `:media` would mean `:media`'s tests depending on the module that depends on `:media` and re-creating that arrangement. In `:messaging` the world gets it for free; its own knobs (`ghost.media.crash`, `.seeds`, `.privacy`, `.report`) join the forwarded property list. `:media`'s own unit tests stay module-local (store, reconcile, fakes of the ports). The world runs real `:sync`, `:messaging`, `:media` and `:crypto-bridge` stacks on SQLite files with production pragmas, the real ratchet, seal and media cryptography through the host build `ghost-dm-host` (which gains `ghost-client-media`), a `CiphertextFiles` port on a real temporary directory with a fault-injecting wrapper, and deterministic fakes for the Android-only parts: a fake sandbox that returns canonical JPEGs from a seeded generator and a fake transcoder that emits a golden canonical fMP4 stream (recorded from the real Transformer in S0: `ghost/protocol/test-vectors/media-goldens/media3-1.11.1/api35-emulator/`, with `tfdt` and `mfra`, accepted by the profile checker). **Native randomness is the OS's**: F9's planned seeded `getrandom` backend was never built (F9 §22.16 item 13 and §22.22 item 7, which found no `getrandom_backend = "custom"` anywhere in the tree), so ciphertexts and hashes differ between runs. Every media check is therefore on structures, joins, normalized views and recorded truth, never on bytes a rerun must reproduce: the crash enumeration checks invariants and oracles after every reboot rather than digest equality with a recording run, and the twins compare normalized views (§15.6). Building the backend is Q33 (default: no).
- **Scale and memory.** `ModelRelay` keeps every blob's bytes in the heap (`ModelRelay.kt:98`, `content = HashMap<BlobHash, ByteArray>`), and the JVM test tasks run with a 2 GB heap (`sync/build.gradle.kts:64`). At one image per client per day, N = 300 over 28 days would be about 27 GB across three relays. So the media world (a) fixes a **media rate** per world: 0.5 objects per client per day at the PR scale and 0.3 at the gate scale, 90 % class 0, 9 % classes 1–4, 1 % classes 5–10 (classes above 4 only in scripted objects); (b) stores media blobs in the model relay as **recipes** (object, ordinal) instead of bytes, and re-derives a blob on `get` by re-sealing its chunk: the world's senders seal through the host-only `MediaNative.testSealBeginWithKey`, whose `K_f` and `object_id` come from the world's seeded generator and are recorded in its truth, over content from the seeded fake sandbox or transcoder (≤ 1 ms per 256 KiB chunk, [I], measured in S1); non-media blobs stay as today; (c) keeps a full-bytes relay model for the PR-scale world's end-to-end runs, where the heap allows it. Production code never sees a chosen key (G-M5 keeps the symbol out of shipped libraries).
- **Android.** An emulator job (API 29, 35 and 37 x86_64 images; API 29 booted with `-feature GLESDynamicVersion`, S0-1) runs the real sandbox, Transformer, `GhostMuxer`, Media3 player and file system, and pulls outputs to the host for ExifTool; a `gate` build type variant (F9 §21.13 point 7) allows `run-as` inspection, and `adb root` on the emulator images reaches the system directories of §15.8. Until a runner has KVM, the same scripts run on the owner's Windows emulator (WHPX) as Phase 9's device suites did (F9 §22.32), recorded as local slice evidence and not as gate evidence (D23). A signed physical-device checklist covers what an emulator cannot (hardware codecs, real encoders' SEI and AAC elements, MTE).

### 15.2 Unit, vector and property tests (CS-M1, CS-M2)

1. Vectors of §3.9 in Rust, in the `aead-stream` oracle and in the JDK implementation.
2. **Corruption matrix** over a class-0 and a class-1 object: every single-bit flip at 4 096 sampled positions of every fragment and of the manifest; every fragment swapped with another position, with the same position of another object, duplicated, dropped; the last chunk's flag removed (re-sealed by the test's own key to isolate the flag); a chunk from another object with the same key material except `object_id`; `F` changed in the manifest; a non-zero pad byte; a manifest under a second key crafted to open under ChaCha20-Poly1305 without the commitment (the Albertini et al. multi-key construction, test-only) — each yields its typed code and exposes no content byte (the out buffer is checked zero).
3. **Nonce reuse:** property test over 10⁴ objects with random crashes injected into the sealer's host (the registry finds no repeat); `INDEX_REUSED` on a repeated index; a unit test that `sealBegin` after an abort returns a new `object_id` and key.
4. **Classes and sizes:** every boundary; images always class 0; a 52 428 801-byte input refused.
5. **Descriptor and key slot:** L0 kind 5 round trips with a zero slot; `encrypt` refuses a non-zero slot, a `mediaKeyEnv` on another kind, and a `key_env` whose binding names another contact, role, object or manifest; **both** `decrypt` (a normal message) and `sessionInbound` (a PreKey first message, and the held replay after a key change) return a zero slot and a `key_env` bound to `(in, sender, object_id, manifest_hash)` that unwraps to the sender's `K_f` (host build); a first message that is media, a media message after a reset and a held media message each reach `media_object` with no key in Kotlin; the F9 L0 vectors gain kind-5 cases.
6. **Walkers:** JPEG and fMP4 canonical profiles against the S0 goldens (`ghost/protocol/test-vectors/media-goldens/`, every file accepted), every refused marker and box type, and, from S0: a `traf` without `tfdt`, a `tfdt` that is not the running sum, a `tfra` entry that does not match its `moof`, two `tkhd` with different matrices, a matrix other than 0°/90°/180°/270°, a coded size other than 1 280 × 720, a `trun` with composition offsets, a `level_idc` other than 31, a profile other than 66 or 100, a High-profile SPS with 4:4:4 or 10-bit, a `colr` other than (1, 1, 1, limited), a VUI full-range flag, a fragment above 16 MiB, a video-only `moov` (S0-2, S0-3, S0-13, S0-20, S0-22) (for `jpegCanonicalize`: an input with APP1 EXIF, APP2 ICC and APP13 IPTC comes out without them, which catches PM-M11), `mfra` consistency, the SEI filter, dimensions and duration derived from the stream (a manifest that disagrees is refused), the canonical geometry for objects and previews (a long edge off the ladder or a short edge not a multiple of 16 is refused), the audio sample entry (48 kHz, two channels), pad zero, an image above class 0 refused.
7. **Request and timer rules (JVM, `:messaging`):** a kind-5 L0 in a request stores the message row and no `media_object`, and no `key_env` byte reaches the database or the WAL (PM-M24); a kind-5 item whose conversation timer changed between `MEDIA-COMMIT` and `MEDIA-READY` is enqueued with the object's `ttl_seconds` and deadline (PM-M25); the `M_c` orphan row appears exactly when c's last upload is over and disappears with a new commit to c (PM-M27); `OWNER-WEEK` on a pre-Phase-11 database adds `M_0` only at the EXPIRING PRF time (PM-M28).
8. **RAM hygiene (JVM with a bitmap fake, and the emulator):** every media bitmap GHOST releases was erased before `recycle()`; every `SharedMemory` region and native buffer is zero when released.

### 15.3 Crash enumeration (the F7 §8.3 and F9 §15.3 method, extended to file operations)

Every media transaction runs under the harness that crashes at every SQL statement **and** at every `CiphertextFiles` call (before and after `write`, `force`, `setTimes` (S0-12), directory sync, `delete`, `list`), reboots, checks structural invariants at once, runs to quiescence and checks everything. File faults include "an unsynced write is lost" at the reboot, which is what a power loss does.

| Scenario | What runs | Invariants checked after every reboot |
|---|---|---|
| SCN-MA | preparation, `MEDIA-COMMIT` | no committed object with a missing or wrong-hash file; no `key_env` without all `F + 1` bound rows; orphans only while preparing; the nonce registry holds no repeat; a restart never resumes a sealer |
| SCN-MB | `MEDIA-FEED`, outcomes, resends, `MEDIA-READY`, the deadline, user retries, the descriptor `SEND`, the view-once sender cleanup; run on a class-0 **and a class-10 object (805 ops)** with scripted INDETERMINATE and FAILED outcomes | **per ordinal:** op ids unique; every released outcome maps to exactly one `(object, ordinal, retry_gen, resend_count)`; ≤ 2 resends per ordinal and retry generation; an ordinal acknowledged (`sent`, `degraded`, final `indeterminate`) is never enqueued again, also after a retry; ≤ 32 open ordinals per object and ≤ 64 per device; **per object:** the descriptor enqueued at most once per SEND attempt and only when every ordinal is final and none `failed`; FAILED blocks it; `expire_at_minute` never increases and is lowered exactly at READY or the deadline; the view-once cleanup runs after any final descriptor outcome other than FAILED; the descriptor op's TTL and deadline equal the object's, also when the conversation's timer changed during the upload (R-MT4); once c's last upload is over, c's `media_upload` rows are gone and `dm_orphan_namespace(M_c)` exists, and a new commit to c removes it |
| SCN-MC | `MEDIA-INGEST`, `MEDIA-BIND`, `MEDIA-VERIFY`, `RECV` through `decrypt` **and** `sessionInbound` (a first message that is media, and one in a request), the held replay | no blob consumed without a durable file (unless below the free-space floor, counted); no `media_object` and no `key_env` byte for a descriptor that arrived in a request; every `bound` row names an existing `media_blob`; a `media_blob` is deleted only when no fragment row names it; a hash named by two objects stays bound to both until each resolves; `complete` only with `F + 1` bound rows; `verified` only after a full pass; no L0 with a non-zero slot ever reaches Kotlin |
| SCN-MD | `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN`, the checkpoint, display, `VIEW-ONCE-CLOSE`, lock, restart | `oracle_displayed` has at most one row per view-once object; `key_env` is NULL whenever a display happened; **the raw `.db` and `-wal` hold no byte of the old `key_env` when the handle is returned** (unless the checkpoint reported busy three times, which the run records and counts); a `viewing` row at start becomes `viewed` |
| SCN-ME | `SWEEP` with media, reconcile, `MEDIA-GC`, the `incomplete` and `corrupt` paths, the `M_c` orphan removal | no row or `key_env` of an expired message's object after the commit, and no `key_env` byte in the raw `.db` or `-wal` after the checkpoint; no file after the next reconcile (except blobs another live object names); pooled blobs gone after their day; an orphaned `M_c` has no `sync_namespace` row or credential after the sweep that removes it |
| SCN-MF | migration M | a crash at any statement leaves v5 (or the previous version) with every row and trigger, which migrates again; the v5 → M object-set test (F9 G-9's `sqlite_master` comparison) |

Scale: `media-exit-gate` enumerates every crash point of one scripted run per scenario (both journal modes) plus 1 000 seeded worlds with random crashes, split into **8 shards of at most 180 minutes each** (the `entitlement-exit-gate` pattern of 12 shards; Phase 9's `t2-exit-gate` hit its 240-minute timeout four times, so no single job may approach it); the class-10 SCN-MB run is sampled (every crash point of the first and last 64 ops, 1 000 random points in between). The PR variant samples.

### 15.4 Seeded worlds (functional)

Worlds of 2–6 clients across week boundaries, with: relays that withhold, delay or corrupt fragments; a relay that lies short on expiry; allowance exhaustion; a card holder flooding `M` by deposits and by its own write capability; **a card holder that lists `M` and names another sender's fragment hashes in its own manifest, before, during and after the victim's upload** (hash squatting); a first message that is media (PreKey, function 14), media after a reset and a held media message after a key change; **media in a request from an unknown card holder, then accepted, rejected or expired** (no key kept in any branch, D28); **a timer change during an upload, 7 d → 1 h and 1 h → 7 d** (R-MT4); **one file sent to three contacts in one session** (fan-out, declared and counted, L9-M14); recipients offline 3 h, 3 d and 20 d; a message deleted by its user during upload; an upload that reaches its deadline; `FAILED` and INDETERMINATE fragment ops with user retries; objects of every class; view-once; 5-minute, 1-hour, 7-day and off timers. Oracles: every object whose descriptor is displayed is either `verified` byte-identical to the sender's content or in a declared loss (`incomplete` past its relay expiry because every relay of the set withheld or lost a blob, because the allowance was exhausted, or because a flood evicted its pooled fragments before its descriptor arrived); `corrupt` only for an object whose **own** sender was modified (a manifest that does not open or disagrees, a chunk that fails), never for an honest object because another object named its hashes (relays cannot alter hash-checked blobs, and ownership is decided by each object's AEAD, D25); an object sent in a request never opens on the recipient; every blob of an object and its descriptor carry one TTL; no duplicate objects; resumption without re-fetching a stored blob (the relay-side `get` counts).

### 15.5 T25 for media (the EP-M suite)

For each timer × path (sender uploaded, sender failed (FAILED outcome, and deadline reached), sender deleted by its user during upload, view-once sender after a final SENT, DEGRADED and INDETERMINATE descriptor, recipient awaiting, fetching, verified-unread, verified-displayed, incomplete, corrupt, view-once opened, view-once unopened, media in a request accepted, rejected and expired): advance the virtual clock to the §11.1 bound + 1 min, sweep, reconcile, then assert (1) logically: no `media_object`, `media_upload`, `media_fragment` or `media_outbound_op` row; (2) physically: the raw `.db` and `-wal` contain neither the plaintext canary nor `key_env` bytes, no fragment's bytes after the later of the bound and the object's op deadline (frozen outbox payloads, F9 §10.3), and `noBackupFilesDir/m/` contains no file of the object; (3) the WAL is 0 bytes after the checkpoint; **(3b) right after `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN` returns its handle, and right after every other transaction that nulls or deletes a `key_env`, the raw `.db` and `-wal` hold no byte of the old `key_env`** (mutant MC-M19 removes the checkpoint); (4) a clock stepped back one day does not delay deletion; (5) every captured media store's TTL, and its descriptor's, is the bucket of the timer at the object's commit; (6) the preview cache holds no entry of the object; (7) on the sender, `M_c` has no `sync_namespace` row once its uploads are over and a sweep ran. A **test destination** (a model channel namespace, §5.6) runs the same suite to keep the engine destination-agnostic.

### 15.6 The media privacy world (T42, T47; extends F9 §15.6)

At the PR scale (N = 60 clients, 14 days, one seed, the media rate of §15.1) in `android`, and at the gate scale (N = 300, 28 days, 5 seed pairs, media blobs as recipes, §15.1) in its own `media-exit-gate` job with a budget of 240 minutes, measured in S9 before the number is fixed (Phase 9's gate-scale privacy world never completed in CI, STATUS; if 5 seed pairs do not fit, the owner chooses between shards and fewer pairs, Q22). Joins over the full effective relay view against the world's ground truth:

| Check | Statement |
|---|---|
| JM1 | every media store is 65 536 B; every object has exactly `F + 1` stores with `F ∈ CLASSES`, **except** objects whose truth records a FAILED outcome, a reached deadline or a user deletion during upload: those have fewer, with no descriptor after them, and each is counted and listed (the `LM` exemptions); an object of a 5-minute conversation is never short because of its timer |
| JM2 | `F = class(content_len)`; every image is class 0; each object has exactly one manifest blob |
| JM3 | exact: no blob hash is shared by two objects of honest senders (including the same file sent to two recipients), and no two objects share `(K_f, object_id)` in the world's truth (so no keystream is shared); sampled: 16-byte windows at 64 random offsets of every media blob, in one hash set over the world, collide across objects only by chance (≤ the birthday bound; an exhaustive window join over every byte is too large at the gate scale) |
| JM4 | every media store's TTL is the bucket of the conversation's timer **at the object's commit**, the same for every blob of the object and for its descriptor deposit into `X` (R-MT4, including objects whose conversation changed its timer during the upload), and no store is made after its op's deadline |
| JM5 | all stores into one `M` at one relay present one scope; no client redeems or registers for another's `M`; every own `M_0` redeem and `register` falls at activation, restore or the client's EXPIRING PRF time, like `X_0`'s (§5.2) |
| JM6 | every `list` and `get` of an `M` is made by its owner (reads confined) |
| JM7 | every `get` on an `M` happens in a work bundle of one of that namespace's pair events, for a hash a listing of that namespace returned (no `get` outside the schedule) |
| NI-M1 | twins: a descriptor delivered vs withheld by the relays of `X` → identical relay views of the recipient |
| NI-M2 | twins: the recipient opens, views and view-once-opens everything vs nothing → identical relay views |
| NI-M3 | twins: an image vs a video of class 0; with vs without caption → identical recipient-side and relay views; **sender A vs sender B → identical projected views**: the events on the recipient's namespaces (`M` and `X`), each stripped of the session and circuit that made it. The full views differ by which client session made the burst, which session co-occurrence reveals (L7-D2, L7-D5) and `LM` declares; revision 2's "identical except the sender's own circuit" ignored that |
| NI-M4 | twins: view-once vs normal objects of equal size → identical relay views on both sides |
| NI-M5 | twins: an identity that never uses media vs one that sends and receives media every day → identical redeem and register traces and an identical **first-page** list schedule (start minute, pair and `limit` of the first list request of every event, T19's STANDARD statement; T27, T31, D11). In STANDARD mode T19 fixes only first pages, and an inbound burst of up to 805 blobs adds continuation pages to `M_0`'s list events, so the full list traces differ by the received volume (declared, LIMITE L2.1 #3); revision 2 claimed identical list traces. The media world has no HIGH mode (F-M4), where T19 fixes the whole sequence |
| NI-M6 | twins **at the pool cap**: a card holder floods the recipient's `M_0` past `POOL_CAP`; in one twin the honest descriptors are delivered, in the other the relays of `X` withhold them → identical relay views of the recipient (fetching never depends on what is pooled or bound; mutant PM-M19 stops ingesting at the cap) |
| NI-M7 | twins: a 5-minute vs a 1-hour timer in one conversation, a class-7 video (289 fragments; a 1-hour bucket admits at most class 8, Q29) uploaded **in background sessions only**, so that it reaches the deadline in both → identical relay views on both sides (the burst is never cut by the timer, D26; mutant PM-M20 starts the countdown at the commit) |

**Normalized views.** A twin comparison compares, per relay, the ordered list of events with their minute, operation, namespace role (own inbox, own media, a contact's media, …), size, TTL, credential scope class and circuit, with blob hashes replaced by their rank of first appearance and payload bytes by their length. Ciphertext differs between twins by construction (different content, the OS's randomness); what the relay can **group** and **time** must not.

The seizure check (T34-style, T45): at day 28, dump both databases, keys and files of every client; try every stored `key_env` (unwrapped with the harness state key) on every archived relay blob of expired or viewed objects **and of objects sent in requests**: 0 opened; control: live objects open.

### 15.7 The T5 corpus and EXIF-zero (T43; emulator and device)

Inputs with a unique canary string in every text field and distinctive numeric values in every numeric field:

| Container | Metadata planted |
|---|---|
| JPEG | EXIF: GPS IFD, Make, Model, Software, BodySerialNumber, LensSerialNumber, DateTimeOriginal, OffsetTime, SubSecTime, ImageUniqueID, MakerNote, an IFD1 thumbnail showing a different picture; XMP (history, face regions with names, GCamera MotionPhoto); IPTC (APP13); ICC (APP2); MPF with an Ultra HDR gain map; COM; APP11 JUMBF/C2PA; a Motion Photo MP4 after EOI; a Samsung SEFT trailer; each of the 8 orientations |
| PNG | eXIf, tEXt, iTXt and zTXt (XMP), tIME, iCCP, alpha |
| WebP | EXIF, XMP, ICCP chunks |
| HEIC / AVIF (**refused as sources**, S0 V-M5) | Exif and XMP items, `thmb`, a depth auxiliary image; the check: `UNSUPPORTED_SOURCE` and no sandbox request (S0-4) |
| Non-MP4 "videos" (S0-21) | a PNG, a JPEG, a WebP, a HEIF, an AVIF and a WebM/Matroska file offered as video; the check: a typed `UNSUPPORTED_SOURCE` and no encoder run |
| MP4 / MOV | `mvhd`/`tkhd`/`mdhd` times; `udta` with `©xyz`, `©mak`, `©mod`, `©swr`, `loci`; `meta`/`keys` with `com.android.version`, manufacturer, model, capture fps, ISO 6709 location; an XMP `uuid` box; a GPMF-style metadata track; H.264 SEI user data with a canary; rotation 90 |
| MP4 **already matching the output** (H.264 High or Main + AAC-LC, ≤ 720p30, SDR, square pixels, below the bitrate budget) | everything of the row above, plus distinctive SPS/VUI values (a non-default colour description and level) and a canary in an AAC data-stream element (DSE) and in a fill element; the check adds: the export named both encoders, the output's SPS/VUI carry GHOST's pinned settings and none of the source's values, and no source NAL unit or AAC frame appears byte for byte in the output (V-M17, PM-M18). **The colour check (S0-13):** the source is BT.601 full range with saturated colour patches (S0's `quad` grid: red, green, blue, yellow, white, black); the test decodes the output into buffers (not a surface), converts it with the output's own colour label and requires every patch within 8 levels per channel of the source's RGB, so a label that does not describe the pixels (S0: API 29's encoder coded BT.601 under a BT.709 label, 40–41 levels off) or a relabel without conversion fails; the same test runs the calibration of §8.3 point 5(d) and asserts its choice per API level (BT.601 on both S0 emulators) |
| **Stream parameters (D29)** | a 1 080 × 2 400 and a 720 × 1 600 PNG screenshot, a 300 × 200 JPEG, a 4 000 × 3 000 photo; a 60 fps and a 24 fps video; audio at 44.1 kHz mono and at 48 kHz 5.1; a 1 920 × 1 080 and a 640 × 480 video; a 4-channel source; a 90°, a 180° and a 270° rotated video and a coded-portrait one; a video without audio; a 45-s 24-fps source (at 24 fps a 1 000-frame IDR interval makes a 41.7-s fragment, S0-2, S0-8); the check: every image's geometry is on the ladder with a short edge rounded to 16, every video is 1 280 × 720 or 720 × 1 280 displayed at ≤ 30 fps (sample count against duration) with 48 kHz stereo audio, its orientation right **by content** (the first frame's colour grid, not only the dimensions), and one audio and one video track (PM-M26; S0-20) |

**Where the corpus comes from (S0a, S0b).** The corpus is built once and committed as test assets (`ghost/android/media/src/androidTest/assets/corpus/`, with a manifest of SHA-256 sums and, per file, the recipe that made it), so CI needs only the ExifTool oracle. Generated files (S0a, on the owner's workstation; Python is not installed, and none of this needs it): ExifTool writes the EXIF, XMP, IPTC, ICC and QuickTime `udta`/`Keys` tags and the XMP `uuid` box; an H.264/AAC encoder run on the host (ffmpeg, with its `h264_metadata` bitstream filter for the SEI user-data canary) makes the base videos, including the matching-source ones; a small test-only Kotlin writer in the host test harness adds what the tools do not write: the `gpmd` metadata track, a Motion-Photo-style MP4 after EOI, an APP11 JUMBF segment with a canary, and AAC frames whose raw data block **starts** with a DSE and a FIL element carrying the canary, followed by a fixed silent channel element and `ID_END` (so no AAC parsing is needed to place them; the silent element is checked by decoding it, [I]). Device captures (S0b, the reference phones of Q31): a real Motion Photo, a Samsung SEFT trailer, an Ultra HDR JPEG with its gain map and, where a phone writes it, C2PA content credentials. **Captures are made with location off or a mock location, and their GPS, serial numbers and owner fields are rewritten to planted values with ExifTool before commit**, reviewed by the owner: the repository must not receive the owner's real coordinates or device serials. Every tool is a host tool run to build fixtures, never shipped or linked (ADR-37, like ExifTool below); the exact tool versions are recorded in the corpus manifest. **Test input path:** the corpus reaches `sendMedia` through a read-only `ContentProvider` declared only in the `androidTest` APK, whose content URIs the instrumentation passes as `MediaSource`s (the app's merged manifest is unchanged, G-M4); on API 29 no picker UI is involved either.

Each goes through `sendMedia` on the emulator (and on the gate devices), is received by a second instance, verified and decrypted; the host then checks: (1) `exiftool -a -G1 -s` (the latest **production** release when S0b pins it, 13.55 of 2026-04-07 at this writing, pinned by SHA-256; revision 2 named 13.59, a development release) lists only an allowlist of structural tags with constant values (the reading of T5 in X-M13); (2) a byte search finds no canary (ASCII, UTF-16LE, UTF-16BE) and no planted GPS rational or ISO 6709 string; (3) orientation goldens hold (pixel comparison within tolerance); (4) nothing follows EOI; the video has no `udta`, `meta`, `uuid` and its times equal the constant; (5) the preview passes the same checks. ExifTool is GPL-or-Artistic ("same terms as Perl"), run only on CI hosts, never shipped or linked (ADR-37 allows CI tools; point 9 is about implementing from AGPL sources, not running a tool).

### 15.8 No-plaintext-cache and Media3 (emulator; the gate's third item)

1. **Sandbox scan.** After a script that sends and receives the corpus, names each conversation on screen and asks for its previews (`setConversationOnScreen`, `mediaPreview`, §6.5), plays every video with 20 random seeks, opens a view-once item, backgrounds, and locks and unlocks by calling `GhostApp.onLock()` and `onUnlock()` directly (they have no caller before Phase 13, `GhostApp.kt:166-185`): pull every app directory (`files`, `cache`, `code_cache`, `no_backup`, `databases`, `shared_prefs`, `app_*`, `/data/user_de/0/<pkg>`, `/sdcard/Android/data/<pkg>`, `/sdcard/Android/media/<pkg>`) and search every file for 64-byte windows at 97 random offsets of every plaintext and preview, **of every picked source file and of the decoded RGBA rows** of every displayed image, and for the canaries: 0 hits. Every file under `m/` is 65 536 B and passes a χ² uniformity test; no other media-looking file exists (magic-byte sniff). **System directories** (emulator images with `adb root`): the same search over `/data/system_ce/0/snapshots` (task snapshots; every media window, including the harness activity, has FLAG_SECURE and on API 33+ `setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)`, so 0 hits is required; mutant PM-M23 drops them), and over `/data/tombstones` and `/data/system/dropbox` after a forced `:mdec` crash, whose content is **recorded, not required to be empty** (L9-M8, V-M20); the search **decodes the text tombstones' hex dumps back to bytes** and also searches `.pb` files and gunzipped dropbox entries (S0-16: a plain byte search of the text form misses every hit). The picker residues are listed per API level (`/data/media/0/**/.thumbnails`, `/data/media/0/.transforms/synthetic`, MediaProvider's `cache/image_manager_disk_cache` and `picker.db`, DocumentsUI's databases; S0-17). **Root search (S0-1):** a marker is touched before the run, and afterwards a root shell lists every regular file under `/data` whose modification **or status-change** time is newer than the marker (toybox: `find /data -xdev -type f \( -newer M -o -newercm M \)`, or `-cmin -N` over the marker's age where toybox has no `-newercm`, as on API 29 (S0); `-newer` alone misses a file whose `mtime` was set to 0, which §10.4 prescribes for GHOST's own files); the listing is reported only if `adbd` answers as root, and it ends with an explicit end line only when `find` exited 0, so neither an unprivileged search nor a failed one can pass for a clean result. Every file it names outside the documented system bookkeeping (ART profiles, `package-dex-usage.pb`, power statistics) fails the scan.
2. **`MediaStore`:** a query for rows whose `OWNER_PACKAGE_NAME` is GHOST returns 0; `dumpsys media_session` shows no GHOST session.
3. **Seek, resume, corruption (FR-7.4).** Seeks to 50 random positions, drawn over the whole object from the manifest's `duration_ms` (never from fragment times: S0's probe drew them from fragment times and degenerated on a one-fragment file), **show the target frame ±1, identified by content, not by timestamp** (every corpus video frame carries its index in luma blocks; S0 V-M2 showed correct timestamps on wrong pictures, up to 91 frames off, without `tfdt`; corrected by S0-2, revision 3 asked only for "within one fragment duration"); mutant MC-M24 (the player built without `FLAG_READ_MFRA_FOR_SEEK_MAP`, so the seek map is unseekable and playback restarts from the beginning) must be caught by that check; playback resumes after `onStop`/`onStart`, and after a process restart in a **host-driven two-part run**: part one (an instrumentation test) starts an upload and a playback and records its state, the host script kills the app's process (`adb shell am force-stop`, or `kill -9` of its PID with `adb root`), and part two (a second instrumentation run) checks that the upload resumes without re-storing an acknowledged blob and that the object plays from the recorded position. Instrumentation dies with the process, which is why Phase 9 deferred its own kill test (F9 §22.17 item 10); this run is new harness work in S7, and if it is not built, the JVM crash enumeration (§15.3) is the resume evidence and this item claims only `onStop`/`onStart`. A corrupted fragment file (one flipped byte, injected on disk after `verified`) makes the reader fail with `HASH_MISMATCH`, the player end with a typed error, not a crash, and the object become `corrupt` (`verified → corrupt`, §7.3; a JVM variant runs in `media-exit-gate`, mutant MC-M22), and nothing plaintext is written.
4. **Logs:** the F9 logcat scan with a random marker line (F9 §22.32) finds no canary across the run, including `:mdec`.
5. **Backup (T4):** `bmgr` and a device-transfer dry run contain nothing of `m/`.
6. **Decoder isolation (T46).** Every strip and decode request of the run reports a distinct PID and a process that is gone after the reply (`dumpsys activity processes`); a STRIP and a DECODE never share a PID (mutant PM-M21 reuses one instance); binding the service on API 29, 35 and 37 connects within the 10-s request timeout of §9.2 with the `GhostApp` early return, and without it gets no callback within that timeout (MC-M23; no speed is asserted, S0-15). **Container refusal (S0-21):** a picked "video" that is really a PNG, a JPEG, a WebP, a HEIF, an AVIF or a WebM/Matroska file gets a typed `UNSUPPORTED_SOURCE` and no encoder runs (S0 V-M14: Media3's default extractors transcoded a WebM file; with `Mp4Extractor` alone every one of these failed with a typed export error on API 29 and 35).
7. **Zero-click and the on-screen signal (T46).** With a decoder spy on the sandbox binding: no decode request while no conversation is named on screen, while the app is in the background, for a blocked contact or for a request; previews only for the named conversation; `ON_STOP` and `onLock()` stop them. The picker and DocumentsUI residues of the run are listed (V-M21, L9-M15).

### 15.9 Mutants (52; each detected against a same-scale control)

Each mutant counts in exactly one report, its **home**: `media-exit-gate` (JVM `MediaMutantDetectionTest` and Rust `media_mutants`; 43 mutants, report line `media mutants: 43 run, 43 detected`), `media-emulator` (7 mutants that need Android, line `media emulator mutants: 7 run, 7 detected`) or `self-test.sh` (2 mutants that are static-gate fixtures, counted in the gate's hit counts). **S0 added MC-M24 (S0-2):** the walker requires a `tfdt` in every `traf`, so "`tfdt` insertion removed" fails at `sealAppend` before any object exists and would never reach the seek test; that case is a Rust walker vector (§15.2 item 6), and the emulator mutant aims at the reader instead. Revision 1 claimed 32 in one JVM report although five of them could only be caught on Android; revision 2 added 14 for the corrected rules; revision 3 adds PM-M24 … PM-M28 and redefines PM-M11, which as written in revisions 1 and 2 ("EXIF copied onto the re-encoded JPEG") could not be detected: the sender's `jpegCanonicalize` drops every APPn before sealing, so the mutation changed nothing observable.

### 15.10 Exit-gate argument

| Gate item (plan:315) | Evidence | Where it runs |
|---|---|---|
| corruption/resume | §15.2 matrix and vectors (Rust and JVM); SCN-MA … SCN-MC (per-ordinal upload state, the class-10 object); seeded worlds incl. hash squatting; §15.8 item 3 (Media3 seek, resume, on-disk corruption) | `rust`, `android`, `media-exit-gate` (JVM); emulator job for item 3 |
| EXIF-zero | §15.7 corpus (committed, with its recipes) through the real pipeline, including sources that already match the output and the stream-parameter row, ExifTool and canaries; walker property tests | emulator job (+ gate devices); `rust` |
| no-plaintext-cache | G-M1, G-M2, G-M3 static gates; §15.8 sandbox and system-directory scans, `MediaStore`, logs, backup, decoder isolation, zero-click; T45 physical scans of the database and WAL | PR gates; emulator job; `media-exit-gate` |
| (also) T25 media, T42–T47, 52 mutants (43 + 7 + 2) | §15.5, §15.6, §15.9 | `android` (`:messaging`'s `MediaWorld`), `media-exit-gate`, emulator job, `self-test.sh` |

**What is not proven without hardware or people (CP-10).** The Android half of the gate needs an emulator job on a runner with KVM, which the project does not have (`dm-emulator.yml` has never run on a runner; `docs/STATUS.md`), or a signed manual run of the same scripts. The `android` CI job itself has been skipped on every run since 2026-09-20, because `android-native` fails at its artifact upload (the CI artifact quota, an owner action, STATUS), so until that is fixed no Android slice can end green in CI and local runs are the slice evidence (D23, Q32). Real encoders' SEI and AAC elements, hardware codecs and MTE need the physical-device checklist on owner-provided phones (Q31). Transfer times need live Tor and, for realism, staging relays (an owner action). The media format is inside the external cryptographic review scope, which has not happened for Phase 9 either. Until all apply, `docs/STATUS.md` records Phase 11 as "implemented and tested locally", and no UI claim is made.

### 16.1 Gates (each with a negative fixture and a `self-test.sh` hit count)

| Gate | Rule | Supports |
|---|---|---|
| **G-M1** `media-no-plaintext-io.sh` | **Two rules with two scopes.** (a) **Sinks**, in every `android/*/src/main` (and the `gate` source set): no reference to `SimpleCache`, `CacheDataSource`, `CacheDataSink`, `CacheWriter`, `DownloadManager`, `DownloadService`, `DownloadHelper`, `DefaultDataSource`, `DefaultHttpDataSource`, `HttpEngineDataSource`, `UdpDataSource`, `AesCipherDataSource`, `AesCipherDataSink`, `FileDataSource`, `InAppMp4Muxer`, `MediaStore`, `MediaScannerConnection`, `getExternalFilesDir(s)` / `.externalCacheDir`, `getExternalCacheDir(s)`, `getExternalMediaDirs`, `Environment.getExternalStorage`, `createTempFile`, `getCacheDir()` / `.cacheDir`, `getCodeCacheDir()` / `.codeCacheDir`, `openFileOutput`, `FileProvider`, `ACTION_SEND`, `ClipData`, `BigPictureStyle`, `setLargeIcon`, `MediaSession`, **`takePersistableUriPermission`** (S0-7: API 29's DocumentsUI offers a read **and write** persistable grant on the picked file), **`StandaloneDatabaseProvider`, `DatabaseProvider`** (S0-5: `media3-database` ships as a hard transitive dependency; its provider is a SQLite writer for caches and downloads) (the Kotlin property forms are matched after a `.`, so a parameter named `cacheDir`, as in `TorRelayTransport.create`, is not a hit); `Transformer` only in `GhostTranscoder`. (b) **File writers**, in every `android/*/src/main`: `FileOutputStream`, `RandomAccessFile`, `FileWriter`, `PrintWriter(File…)`, `Files.write`, `Files.writeString`, `Files.newOutputStream`, `Files.newBufferedWriter`, `Files.copy`, `Files.move`, `FileChannel.open` with a write option, `AtomicFile`, Kotlin's `File.writeBytes`, `writeText`, `appendBytes`, `appendText`, `outputStream()`, `bufferedWriter()`, `printWriter()`, `copyTo`, `copyRecursively`, `ContentResolver.openOutputStream`, `openFileDescriptor`/`openAssetFileDescriptor` with a mode other than `"r"`, `ParcelFileDescriptor.open` with a write mode and `openFileOutput` appear only in files listed in `scripts/gates/file-writer-allowlist.txt`, each line a reviewed exception with its reason: today `identity/.../AndroidKeystoreWrapper.kt` (the sealed key envelopes, `:97-99`) and, from Phase 11, `media/.../store/CiphertextFiles.kt`. Revision 2 applied a writer rule to all of `src/main` with `CiphertextFiles` as the only exception, which the Keystore wrapper already breaks, and missed Kotlin's writers | T44 |
| **G-M2** Gradle dependency rule | allowed Media3 artifacts: `media3-common`, `-datasource`, `-database` (a hard transitive dependency of `-datasource` and `-exoplayer`, S0-5; its classes are G-M1 sinks), `-decoder`, `-extractor`, `-container`, `-exoplayer`, `-transformer`, `-effect`, `-muxer` (and `-ui` from Phase 13); forbidden: `media3-session`, `-inspector` (it holds `FrameExtractor`), `-datasource-okhttp`, `-datasource-cronet`, `-datasource-rtmp`, `-exoplayer-hls`, `-exoplayer-dash`, `-exoplayer-smoothstreaming`, `-exoplayer-rtsp`, `-exoplayer-ima`, `-exoplayer-workmanager`, `-cast`; `com.google.android.gms` stays denied | T44, ADR-06 |
| **G-M3** `media-decoder-confinement.sh` | **source rule:** `ImageDecoder`, `BitmapFactory`, `BitmapRegionDecoder`, `ExifInterface`, `MediaMetadataRetriever`, `HeifDecoder`, `ThumbnailUtils`, `loadThumbnail`, `setImageURI`, `Drawable.createFromStream`, `Drawable.createFromPath`, `Icon.createWithContentUri`, `Icon.createWithFilePath`, `Icon.createWithData`, `AnimatedImageDrawable`, `Movie` and Media3's `FrameExtractor` referenced only in `org.ghost.media.sandbox`; **bytecode rule:** the same method and class references, read from the compiled `org.ghost` classes of the release variant (`javap -c` over the class jars, the dex listing of V-M14 where R8 renamed nothing of GHOST's), appear only in `org.ghost.media.sandbox`, so an alias, an import or a call through a helper does not slip past a name list; library code (Media3's own bitmap loaders) is outside the rule because no GHOST class calls it, which the same bytecode listing shows (the release dex as a whole legitimately contains `DataSourceBitmapLoader`, `ExifInterface`, `ImageAssetLoader`, `DefaultExtractorsFactory` and every extractor: R8 keeps them through Media3's builders, S0 V-M14). **Configuration rule (S0-21):** every Media3 instance GHOST builds (the player of §10.2 and Transformer of §8.3; there is no preview player, §6.2) is constructed with GHOST's factories only: `ExoPlayer.Builder(context, renderersFactory, mediaSourceFactory)` with a renderers factory that builds exactly one `MediaCodecVideoRenderer` and one `MediaCodecAudioRenderer`; extractors factories that return only `FragmentedMp4Extractor` (received objects) or only `Mp4Extractor` (picked sources); `DefaultAssetLoaderFactory` with GHOST's `MediaSource.Factory` and a `BitmapLoader` that refuses every type; no `EditedMediaItem` image duration. ## 17. Slices, effort, exit-gate checklist

### 17.1 Slices (the Q defaults apply unless the owner objects)

**Evidence per slice.** A slice ends green in CI where CI can run it: the `rust` job, the static gates and the JVM jobs. The `android` job has been skipped since 2026-09-20 (the CI artifact quota, an owner action), and no runner has KVM, so until the owner fixes both (Q32) a slice's Android and emulator evidence is a **local run** (the owner's Windows machine, WHPX emulator), recorded in STATUS with its commit as Phase 9 recorded its device suites (F9 §22.32). Local runs are slice evidence, never gate evidence (D23).

### 17.2 Effort (calibrated on Phase 9)

**Calibration.** Phase 9's design estimated about 10–12 weeks (F9 §17). Its slices S1 … S14 landed between 2026-09-16 and 2026-09-24, nine calendar days of parallel agent sessions (`git log`). What did **not** finish is the gate evidence: on 2026-09-25 STATUS lists 15 of 57 correctness and 15 of 40 privacy mutants built and run (the gate needs 97), a gate-scale privacy world that never completed in CI, an emulator job that never ran on a runner, and a two-phone checklist not executed. Revision 1's "7–9 weeks for 5–6 people, ≈ 31 person-weeks" was a team model with no calibration and cited STATUS for slice overruns it does not contain; it is withdrawn.

**Revision 4 (S0, S0-24).** The S0 evidence added work to nearly every slice, and its own effort column (16.25 days, labelled "S1 implementation days") mostly landed elsewhere. The table below is the single calibration: revision 3's estimate per slice, the S0 corrections that land in that slice with their effort (§21.3 lists every correction and its sections), and the sum. It replaces revision 3's table and D24's figure.

| Slice | Revision 3 | S0 corrections landing here (agent-days) | Revision 4 (agent-days) | CI per run | Owner dependency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S0a | 3–4 | spent on the evidence (2026-09-25 … 10-04, ≈ 4); still open: verification metadata, G-L2, G-M7, the generated corpus, ADRs (1.5–2.5); API 37 if Q47 says so (+1) | 5.5–6.5 (≈ 4 spent) | — | none (local emulator); Q47 for the API 37 image |
| S0b | 1–2 | S0-8 tooling 0.5, S0-11 0.25, S0-14 0.25, S0-22 device goldens 1, new checklist lines (mount options, Play services picker, residue retention, the retry on hardware, HDR) 0.5–1 | 3.5–5 | — | reference phones (Q31), onion relay / staging, ExifTool pin, review of the device captures |

With S1 ∥ S3 ∥ S4-sync after S0a, S4-owner after S2, and S7 ∥ S8 (revision 2 serialised S3 and S4 behind S2, which neither needs in full), that is **about 4–6 calendar weeks to "implemented and tested locally"** at Phase 9's parallelism, and longer at a slower agent pace. **This no longer fits the plan's 3–4 weeks** (revision 3's 2–3 weeks did; the overrun comes from the S0 corrections, chiefly S5's and S7's), so Q11's and Q22's rationales are restated (§19), and the owner decides whether to accept the longer phase or cut scope (Q22). **The exit gate has no date this design can promise:** it needs the KVM runner (or a signed run of the same scripts), the CI artifact quota fixed so the `android` job runs again, two reference phones and a person for the checklist, and staging relays for V-M10, the same owner actions that keep Phase 9's gate open; after they exist, expect one to two weeks of CI and fix rounds (Phase 9's gate evidence took longer than its code). If a gate date is binding, Q22 lists the scope options.

### 17.3 Exit-gate checklist (all on the closing commit)

1. S0a's and S0b's V-M results recorded in Appendix C; no open S0 fallback.
2. `rust`, `android`, `sync-exit-gate`, `t2` PR variant green **on a runner** (not skipped).
3. `media-exit-gate`: every SCN-M scenario at every crash point (the class-10 SCN-MB sampled as §15.3 states); 1 000 worlds; the gate-scale privacy world with JM1–JM7 = 0 (JM1's exemptions listed) and NI-M1–NI-M7 equal; `media mutants: 43 run, 43 detected`; the seizure line; `media-report-check.sh` passes.
4. `media-emulator` on API 29, 35, 37 **run on a runner** (or a signed manual run of the same scripts, recorded as such): §15.7 and §15.8 pass; `media emulator mutants: 7 run, 7 detected`.
5. The physical-device checklist steps for media (two phones: send and receive an image and a video in both directions, a matching-source video, view-once, expiry at 5 minutes, sandbox and snapshot scans on a real device, a real encoder's SEI and AAC elements, sync-sample positions and SPS under GHOST's encoder settings (the device goldens of S0-22), its bitrate overshoot in both modes, whether a lower bitrate shrinks hard content and how often the 15-fps retry succeeds (the class cap is enforced while writing, §6.2; there is no margin to check against), its colour calibration and the colour grid by content, its preparation time for a 160-s source (R-MT2), `/data` mount options and file times, which picker `PickVisualMedia` opens on an API 29–30 phone with Play services, the picker residues and their retention on a running phone, HDR tone-mapping), signed.
6. G-M1 … G-M10 and G-L2 in `run-all.sh` with fixtures; PM-M10 and PM-M14 counted in `self-test.sh`.
7. Appendix B applied to INVARIANTS (T5 replaced, T42–T47, T10, T33 and T35 changes), THREAT_MODEL (AS-4, §5, §6, §9), LIMITE (L9), the plan erata, STATUS, the Phase 7 and Phase 9 designs' forward notes; the numbering of Q38 agreed with the Phase 10 design.
8. The review rounds' findings corrected or recorded as owner decisions (§21 for the design, §22 for the implementation).
9. ADR-44 … 47 decided by the owner or applied under the default rule.
10. No UI claim (CP-10); STATUS says what ran where.

## 18. Risks

| ID | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-M1 | The implementation fits the plan's 3–4 weeks but the exit gate does not, because its evidence waits on owner actions and CI capacity (as Phase 9's does); Phase 13's dependency on 11 slips | high / medium | §17.2 calibration; S0a first and S0b off the critical path; Q22 scope options; S-CH outside the phase |
| R-M2 | Media3 internals change between versions (muxer writes, extractor seek logic), breaking the canonical profile or seeking | medium / high | exact pin; G-M8 goldens; `GhostMuxer` owns metadata; the walker fails closed |
| R-M3 | The isolated process cannot decode HEIC/AVIF, or `SharedMemory` behaves differently on API 29 | **happened (S0 V-M5):** HEIF/AVIF go through `media.player`, which isolated apps may not reach; `SharedMemory` works on API 29 | HEIC/AVIF refused as sources (§6.1); Q39 offers a main-process decode through the media server; JPEG, PNG, WebP sources suffice for P0 |
| R-M4 | Transfers are too slow over Tor with a client per RPC; background uploads miss short-TTL deadlines | high / medium | media lane; foreground transfers; Phase 12 pooling (F-M2); no timing claims |
| R-M5 | Allowance exhaustion by a hostile card holder blocks a user's media for a week | medium / low | L9-M4; Q20; card rotation (Phase 13) |
| R-M6 | Device encoders emit SEI or stream fields the walker refuses, so video sending fails on some phones | medium / medium | the SEI filter in `GhostMuxer`; V-M12 across the reference devices; the walker's allowlist from goldens of several devices |
| R-M7 | No KVM runner: the Android half of the gate never runs in CI | high / high | signed manual runs of the same scripts; the owner's runner action (shared with Phase 9) |
| R-M8 | Phase 10, or a Phase 9 fix migration (LIMITE L5's TIMER_SET and control-retry columns), claims the same ADR, invariant, LIMITE-section or migration numbers, or rebuilds `dm_outbound_item` separately | **happened** (the Phase 10 design uses ADR-40 … 43, T36–T41, L8 and migrations 6–7) / low once reserved | numbers reserved now (Q38: ADR-44 … 47, T42–T47, L9, the migration after Phase 10's); rebuilds copy the tables as they are at merge; Q27 folds the Phase 9 columns into M |
| R-M9 | Phase 9's open gate or a Phase 9 defect (e.g. L5's TIMER_SET bucket) leaks through the descriptor | medium / medium | media adds no new DM mode; the descriptor is an ordinary 4 KiB user send; DM claims wait for Phase 9's gate |
| R-M10 | A platform image-decoder exploit in the sandbox escapes the isolated process | low / high | isolation limits it to a platform privilege-escalation chain; canonical JPEG only; patched devices; MTE where available |
| R-M11 | Plaintext in the JVM heap during playback is read by a memory-scraping attacker with root | low / medium | foreground only; release at lock; declared (L9-M7) |
| R-M12 | Forced automatic download costs users data, more than the honest allowance when a card holder floods with its own tokens (§5.3) | medium / medium | allowance; Phase 13 pause switch; Phase 12 owner-bound rule; Q4 |
| R-M13 | `t2-exit-gate` (already 222 of 240 minutes on one pair, F9 §22.24) exceeds its timeout with a third owner namespace | medium / medium | shard it or raise the timeout (owner decision, as for `messaging-exit-gate`'s 240 → 480) |
| R-M14 | One isolated process per decode is too slow for a conversation screen full of previews | medium / low | S0 (V-M18): 0.16–2.5 s per start at the median on loaded emulators, not a device figure; Q40's criterion on the reference phone; a pre-bound spare instance first, Q25's fallback (one instance per sender and screen for previews only, declared weaker) second |
| R-M15 | Owner actions (KVM runner, CI artifact quota, reference phones, staging relays, ExifTool pin) are not done, so the gate cannot close | high / high | S0b and the gate-only items are off the implementation path; local runs recorded as slice evidence; Q31, Q32 |
| R-M16 | A device's encoder emits stream fields or AAC elements outside the goldens recorded on the emulator, so sends fail on that device | **high / high** (S0: the goldens come from two Baseline-only software encoders, while Media3 1.11.1 makes every High-capable phone encoder write High profile, S0-22) | the walker accepts the High profile as specified in §8.3 (SPS walked in full); level, B-frames, key-frame interval and frame rate pinned by GHOST; device goldens in S0b before any distributed build sends video from that device class; the walker fails closed with a typed error; the allowlist grows by recorded goldens only (G-M8); the colour calibration of §8.3 point 5(d) keeps a phone's label true to its pixels or refuses video there; Q44 offers Baseline everywhere through GHOST's own encoder format |
| R-M17 | An encoder overshoots its bitrate on hard content, so a long video fails with `OVERSIZE` | **happened on the emulators** (S0 V-M22: +29 % to +169 % with a noise patch, +2 442 % to +4 232 % on full-frame noise; the software encoders' rate control saturates at their quantiser cap of 40 and a lower requested bitrate does not shrink the output) / medium | the limits of §6.1 bound only the source's duration; the class cap is enforced while writing (`OVERSIZE` cancels at once); the retry rule of §6.2 (Q41); VBR by default; the phones' encoders measured in S0b |
| R-M18 | A platform encoder or `Presentation` does not produce the canonical geometry, frame rate or audio format on some API level, so video sends fail there | **happened** (S0 V-M24: with portrait encoding on, API 29's encoder produced 612 × 1 088; `ChannelMixingMatrix.createForConstantGain` exists only for 1–2 input channels) / medium | portrait encoding off (landscape coding with a rotation, the only coding that yields the canonical geometry on API 29 and 35); explicit down-mix matrices for 3–8 channels; V-M24 on API 37 and the reference phones; the walker fails closed; Q37's alternative (declare the parameter) for the affected one |
| R-M19 | The emulators' software codecs fail intermittently under host load (S0: one 160-s export stalled without an error; two exports failed at `configure()` of a codec already released, once the AAC and once the H.264 encoder), so emulator-job runs are flaky | medium / low | the stall watchdog of §6.2; one automatic re-run of a failed emulator test case, recorded; a failure that repeats is a finding |
| R-M20 | API 37, the target SDK, was not measured in S0 (no image on the workstation), so a behaviour change there is found only in the emulator job or on a phone, after S1 | medium / medium | Q47: run the S0a checks on an API 37 image before S1 (the image download needs the lead's or owner's go-ahead) or accept the late re-plan risk |

## 19. Owner questions (the proposed default applies unless the owner objects)

**Owner decision (2026-10-04).** The owner approved **Q1 … Q38 with their proposed defaults and the five questions of the S0 evidence, Q-S1 … Q-S5 (Q39 … Q43 here), with theirs**: ADR-44 … ADR-47 approved; still images and video only; `M_i` media namespace paid by the recipient; automatic download; ×1.5 size classes; canonical JPEG and H.264 + AAC re-encoding; native key custody; view-once in the gate; files for the ciphertext store; the calibrated estimate and the slice order with no scope cut; HEIC/HEIF and AVIF sources refused (Q39 a); one fresh isolated process per request with the p90 ≤ 250 ms criterion (Q40); the class cap enforced while writing with one 15-fps retry (Q41); the picker residues declared (Q42); the four annotation-only artifacts excluded if S1 shows the build unchanged (Q43). **Not part of that answer:** Q44 … Q48 (Q-S6 … Q-S10), raised by the reviews of the S0 evidence; their defaults stand under this section's rule unless the owner objects (Q44 High profile where Media3 chooses it, Q45 rotation declared, Q46 the 16 MiB fragment bound accepted, Q48 colour label (a)), and **Q47 (the API 37 emulator image, a system-image download whose size `sdkmanager --list` reports before it starts) waits for an explicit go-ahead**; S1 does not depend on it.

**Owner decision (2026-10-05).** **Q47** was answered with the download (done: the API 37 image is installed, S0a was run on it, §22.1). **Q-S11 (= Q49, S0-26: the image's hardware AVC decoder cannot be fed by an app)** is answered with its default **(a): `GhostTranscoder` retries once with a software decoder** (and the emulator job boots with `-feature -HardwareDecoder`). **Q27** is answered **fold**: Phase 9's open schema fixes (the TIMER_SET bucket column of `dm_outbound_item`, i.e. R-T3's pending path, and the attempts column of `dm_scheduled`, LIMITE L5) go into migration M's rebuild, by amending M in place while it is unshipped (slice S3b after S3 merges, with the Phase 9 test changes of `faza9-dm.md` §22.39 item 8). Q44 … Q48 stay on their defaults.

| # | Question | Proposed default | Pe scurt (RO) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Approve ADR-44 … ADR-47 (Appendix A) as "propus, aplicat"? | **yes** | Aprobarea ADR-44 … ADR-47 |
| Q2 | Content types in Phase 11: still images and video only; no voice notes, audio files, animated images, documents or RAW? | **yes** | Doar imagini statice și video; fără mesaje vocale, fișiere, GIF-uri animate |
| Q3 | DM media in a recipient-owned, card-derived media namespace `M_i` with a shared deposit credential, paid by the recipient (one more token per relay per week for every identity, and one more listened pair per relay, which about doubles each identity's own list traffic, §5.2), rather than in the DM inbox or in sender-paid namespaces? | **`M_i`** | Media stă într-un namespace separat al destinatarului, plătit de el, nu în inbox-ul de text (costă un token și încă o listare periodică per releu) |
| Q4 | Download: automatic on `M`'s schedule (no reflex; forced data use), rather than on descriptor or on tap (both tell relays when media is opened)? A Phase 13 global pause switch is allowed. | **automatic** | Descărcare automată, ca releul să nu afle când deschizi media |
| Q5 | Size classes ×1.5 (11 classes, all images in one class, ≤ 59 % overhead) rather than Padmé (over byte lengths, its definition: ≈ 110 classes above class 0 after chunk alignment, rounding ≤ 3.1 %; over fragment counts: ≈ 55 classes, ≤ 12.5 %; §3.6) or powers of two (7 classes, ≤ 100 %)? | **×1.5** | Clase de dimensiune din 1,5 în 1,5; toate pozele arată la fel |
| Q6 | Images always re-encoded to baseline JPEG, ≤ 2 048 px on the long edge (the ladder of Q37), ≤ 1 MiB (quality lowered as needed); transparency composited on white? | **yes** | Pozele se re-encodează JPEG, maximum 2 048 px și 1 MiB |
| Q7 | Video always transcoded to H.264 + AAC, 720p (Q37), ≈ 2 Mbit/s, ≤ 50 MiB, in fragmented MP4, with sources up to 160 s (≈ 2 min 40 s: the byte budget at the profile's rate with a 20 % margin; S0 V-M22 showed that no margin holds on hard content, so the limit bounds the source's duration only and the class cap is enforced while writing, Q41)? | **yes** (1080p would halve the maximum duration) | Video re-encodat la 720p, maximum ~2 minute și 40 de secunde |
| Q8 | Media written to the conversation's 3-relay set like DMs, rather than a 2-relay set (−33 % upload, less availability)? | **3 relays** | Media pe 3 relee, ca mesajele |
| Q9 | Image decoding in an isolated process for sent and received images; HEIC/AVIF sources accepted only if they decode there, otherwise refused (not decoded in the main process)? | **yes** — **answered by S0 (V-M5): they never decode there** (the platform's HEIF/AVIF path needs `media.player`, which isolated apps may not reach), so they are refused; Q39 replaces the alternative | Decodarea imaginilor într-un proces izolat; HEIC nu merge acolo, deci e refuzat (vezi Q39) |

Its cost: about 1 of S8's 1–2 agent-days (`VIEW-ONCE-OPEN`/`-CLOSE`, the sender cleanup, SCN-MD), the view-once paths of EP-M, NI-M4, and mutants MC-M6, MC-M20 and PM-M8 in the gate report; out of the gate, the same work lands in S8 without gating (Q22's first cut) | **yes** (view-once costs about one agent-day; revision 4's estimate exceeds the plan's duration for other reasons, the S0 corrections, so the date question is Q22's, §17.2) | „Vezi o singură dată” în poarta fazei (lărgește poarta planului, cost ~1 zi); cheia se șterge înainte de afișare |

| Q35 | Treat AAC raw data blocks as opaque (declared: a modified sender can put bytes in AAC data elements, which reach only the out-of-process decoder), rather than write an AAC element parser? | **declare, do not parse** | Blocurile AAC nu se parsează; limita se declară |
| Q36 | Media in a **request** from an unknown card holder (F9 §12.3): download it (no reflex) but keep no key, so the object can never be opened, even after the request is accepted (the sender must send it again; D28), rather than keep the key for the request's seven days (a stranger with my card link could leave openable media on my device that I never chose to see, which a seized device then holds)? | **keep no key** | Media primită într-o cerere de la un necunoscut nu se poate deschide niciodată; după acceptare, expeditorul o retrimite |
| Q37 | **Canonical stream parameters** (D29): images leave with a long edge of 512, 1 024 or 2 048 px and a short edge rounded to 16 px; video exactly 1 280 × 720 or 720 × 1 280 (bars added), at most 30 fps, SDR, 48 kHz stereo AAC; rather than keep the source's exact dimensions, frame rate, sample rate and channel count and declare them (a screenshot's size names the screen and often the phone model)? | **canonicalise** (if S0a shows a platform cannot produce one of them, that one parameter is declared for that platform, R-M18) | Dimensiuni, cadre pe secundă și sunet fixe, ca fișierul să nu dezvăluie modelul telefonului |
| Q38 | **Numbering:** the Phase 10 design (proposed, branch `p10-design`) keeps ADR-40 … ADR-43, T36–T41, LIMITE L8 and migrations 6 and 7; Phase 11 takes ADR-44 … ADR-47, T42–T47, LIMITE L9 and the first free migration after Phase 10's, reserved now (this revision already uses them) rather than renumbered at merge? | **yes** (Phase 10 comes first in the plan, and channel media depends on it) | Faza 10 păstrează ADR-40 … 43 și T36–T41; Faza 11 folosește ADR-44 … 47, T42–T47 și L9 |
| Q39 | **HEIC/HEIF and AVIF sources** (S0-4): they cannot be decoded in the isolated process (S0 V-M5: the platform decodes them through `media.player`, which isolated apps may not reach; API 29 has no AVIF decoder at all). (a) Refuse them as sources (the user sees a message); (b) decode only sniffed HEIF/AVIF sources with a main-process `ImageDecoder`, whose parsing and decoding run in the platform's media processes (the trust class GHOST already accepts for video), then scale, compress and canonicalise exactly as `STRIP_IMAGE` does, with a named G-M3 exception for that one call site; (c) relax D17 further? | **(a) refuse** ((b) is the alternative the evidence supports) | Pozele HEIC/AVIF nu se pot decoda în procesul izolat: se refuză (implicit) sau se decodează prin serverul media al sistemului |
| Q40 | **Isolated-process criterion** (S0-14): accept "the p90 of bind + 320-px decode + reply ≤ 250 ms on the reference mid-range phone" for one fresh process per preview; if the phone misses it, first one pre-bound spare instance, then Q25's fallback for previews only? (S0 measured 0.16–2.5 s at the median on loaded emulators, not a device figure) | **accept** | Un proces izolat nou pentru fiecare previzualizare, dacă pe telefonul de referință durează sub 250 ms (p90) |
| Q41 | **Oversize video** (S0-18): hard content overshoots 2 Mbit/s on the emulators by +29 % to +169 % with a noise patch (up to +4 232 % on synthetic noise), and a lower requested bitrate does not shrink it (VBR ignored it on API 35; both rate controls sit at their quantiser cap), while 15 fps cut it by 20–48 %. (a) Keep the 160-s and 85-s source limits, enforce the class cap while writing and retry once at 15 fps when the projection is at most twice the cap (the retried video plays at 15 fps, declared); (b) no retry (`TOO_LARGE` at once); (c) also lower the limits? VBR by default; CBR only on an encoder that S0b measures better in it | **(a)** | Video prea mare: limitele rămân, plafonul clasei se verifică în timpul scrierii și se reîncearcă o dată la 15 cadre/s |
| Q42 | **Picker residues** (S0-17): the system picker keeps thumbnails of the pictures outside GHOST on both measured levels, and on API 29 also GHOST's package name with the folder and time of its last pick, a per-file pick counter and thumbnails of every picture shown (in shared storage); they survive reboots. Declare only (L9-M15), show a one-time notice before the first pick, or refuse picking on API 29? | **declare** | Urmele selectorului de poze al sistemului: doar declarate (implicit), un avertisment unic sau refuz pe API 29 |
| Q43 | **Annotation-only artifacts** (S0-5): exclude `jsr305`, `checker-qual`, `error_prone_annotations` and `j2objc-annotations` in `:media`'s Gradle file if `:app` still builds and R8's output is unchanged (checked in S1), rather than allowlist them in G-L2? | **exclude, if clean** | Cele patru artefacte doar cu adnotări aduse de Media3 se exclud, dacă build-ul rămâne identic |
| Q44 | **H.264 profile** (S0-22): Media3 1.11.1 raises the AVC profile to High on every High-capable encoder (most phones) and keeps the requested level. Accept High at level 3.1 there and Baseline elsewhere (the walker accepts both; the profile is one more device-class bit, declared in L9-M9), rather than build the encoder's `MediaFormat` in GHOST's own encoder factory for Baseline everywhere (more GHOST code to keep in step with Media3, a less efficient stream on phones)? | **accept** (Media3 with GHOST's pins) | Profilul H.264 High pe telefoanele care îl suportă (ales de Media3), cu nivelul fixat de GHOST |
| Q45 | **Rotation** (S0-20): the source's rotation (0°, 90°, 180°, 270°) passes through to the recipient (S0, API 29 and 35), which usually tells the front camera from the back one. Declare it (L9-M9), rather than normalise every output to 0° or 90° (a 180° pre-rotation effect for 180° and 270° sources; not measured in S0, about 0.5 agent-day in S5 plus its emulator check)? | **declare** | Rotația sursei ajunge la destinatar (camera din față sau din spate): declarată, nu normalizată |
| Q46 | **Fragment bound** (S0-2): a fragment above 16 MiB fails the send (`FRAGMENT_TOO_LARGE`); with an encoder that writes an IDR only every 1 000 frames (API 29's emulator encoder; S0b's V-M12 finds a phone that does the same) that happens only for sources below about 16 fps. Accept that restriction, with no duration bound? | **accept** | Un fragment de peste 16 MiB oprește trimiterea; afectează doar sursele sub ~16 cadre/s pe encodere care ignoră intervalul cadrelor cheie |
| Q47 | **API 37 before S1** (S0-24): API 37, the target SDK, was not measured in S0 (no image on the workstation). Run S0a's checks on an API 37 emulator image before S1 (downloading the image needs the lead's or the owner's go-ahead; about one agent-day), rather than accept that an API 37 behaviour change is found only in the emulator job or on a phone, after S1? | **run before S1** | Verificările S0a se rulează și pe API 37 înainte de S1 (descărcarea imaginii cere acordul) |
| Q48 | **Colour label** (S0-13): the encoder, not GHOST, decides the colour matrix of the coded pixels (S0: API 35's encoder coded whatever was requested, API 29's coded BT.601 whatever was requested, so a fixed BT.709 label was wrong there by up to 41 levels per channel; a BT.601 limited request is coded as labelled on both). (a) Request BT.601 limited and verify it once per process and encoder with a short colour-grid clip, switching to BT.709 for an encoder that codes BT.709 and refusing video where neither matches (the walker accepts both labels); (b) request BT.601 limited without the run-time check, the phones checked in S0b only; (c) a static per-encoder table from S0b's measurements? | **(a)** | Video-ul se etichetează BT.601 (corect pe ambele emulatoare), iar eticheta se verifică pe fiecare encoder la prima trimitere |

## 20. Findings outside Phase 11 (proposed as separate tasks)

| ID | Finding | Proposed owner |
|---|---|---|
| F-M1 | Plan row 12 ("review independent al capturii de metadate") depends on 6, 7, 10, not 11, so no phase reviews media traffic | plan erratum (Q23) |
| F-M2 | Phase 7 creates a relay client per RPC (F7:85); bulk transfers pay two round trips per 64 KiB | Phase 12: connection pooling with a rotation epoch, measured on media |
| F-M3 | Relays have no disk cap beyond allowances × TTL; the FR-5 load and abuse suite should include media volumes | Phase 12 relay hardening |
| F-M4 | The DM privacy world has no HIGH mode (F9 §22.16); the media world inherits the gap | Phase 9 follow-up |
| F-M5 | Channel media fan-out: one 50 MiB post read by 1 000 members is about 50 GB of relay egress | Phase 10 design |
| F-M6 | SQLCipher ignores the no-backup directory request (`docs/STATUS.md`, F9 §22.32 point 5); media keys live in that database | Phase 9 owner decision, unchanged |
| F-M7 | T35's provenance labels do not cover the intro inner layer (F9 §22.16 item 18); media in first messages inherits the gap (§3.8) | Phase 9 follow-up |
| F-M8 | The Phase 7 work lane is one thread for every consumer's fetches and stores (`Session.kt:129`); any bulk consumer can delay DM fetches. Phase 11 adds media slicing and a budget share (§5.5); a general per-consumer fairness rule belongs to the sync track | Phase 12 sync review |
| F-M9 | The proposed Phase 10 design reserves TLV 0x0003 `media_ref` in its POST content (`faza10-forum.md` §5.5) and uses ADR-40 … 43, T36–T41, LIMITE L8 and migrations 6–7; its review should adopt the descriptor contract of §4.3 (the 84 fixed bytes as the TLV value, the POST's text as the caption, the key-slot rule in the MLS crate) and record the numbering of Q38 | Phase 10 design review |
| F-M10 | A request's text content is stored and readable for up to seven days (F9 §12.3), a smaller form of the planting exposure D28 closes for media; whether text in a request should also stay unreadable until accepted is a Phase 9 question | Phase 9 owner decision |
| F-M11 | Phase 9's `dm_conversation_timer` (`timer_version`, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) and `dm_pair_offer_root` (`acknowledged`, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) bound a counter with a bare `<`, so `UPDATE OR REPLACE … SET c = NULL` resets it: `timer_version` 5 → 0 and `acknowledged` 1 → 0 were reproduced on the v8 schema (`node:sqlite` 3.50.2, 2026-10-05), the shape that §22.5 row 28 fixed for the media triggers (`NEW.c IS NULL OR NEW.c < OLD.c`). Defence in depth (a caller needs a statement that G-DM4 refuses), but the document that says "never decreases" is wrong. The audit of the same shape in v6 and v7 (Phase 10: `own_seq`, `sessions`, `unopenable_count`, …) is part of the task | Phase 9 follow-up: a migration 9 or an amendment of M while it has not shipped |

## 21. Review rounds of revisions 2 and 3 (2026-09-25), and the S0 corrections of revision 4 (2026-10-04)

Three adversarial reviewers read revision 1 (`4bd01ec`) through three lenses: protocol and cryptography (`RV-C`), privacy and metadata (`RV-P`), feasibility, testability and scope (`RV-F`). Every finding was checked against the repository at `f244b80` or a primary source before it was accepted; several were raised by two lenses and are handled once.

**What each revision read.** A tooling defect cut each review at 10 000 characters before revision 2's author saw it: the protocol review inside its L6, the privacy review inside its M3, the feasibility review inside its item 10. Revision 2 therefore handled only the findings before those points (§21.1) and said so. **Revision 3 read all three reviews in full** (`p11-review-protocol.md`, 12 788 bytes; `p11-review-privacy.md`, 19 829 bytes; `p11-review-feasibility.md`, 15 408 bytes; kept under `.claude/scratch/reviews/`, outside version control) and checked every finding against the current text. After the cut, the protocol review holds only the rest of L6 (already fixed as RV-C11), its list of facts it checked and found correct, and its sources: nothing new. The privacy review holds the rest of M3 (already fixed as RV-P6, D25) and M4 … M7 and L1 … L8, now RV-P7 … RV-P18. The feasibility review holds the Phase 10 half of item 10 and items 11 … 19, now RV-F10b and RV-F11 … RV-F19, plus its list of facts found correct. Every one of them is in §21.2 with its verification and disposition; **no finding of the three reviews remains unread or unanswered.** Revision 3 also applied Q38's numbering to the whole document, so the identifiers in §21.1 read ADR-44 … 47, T42–T47 and L9 where revision 2 wrote ADR-40 … 43, T36–T41 and L8.

### 21.1 Revision 2 (the findings before the cut)

| ID | Finding (short) | Verified | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RV-C1 = RV-F1 | **High.** The key slot was wrapped only in `decrypt` (13); `sessionInbound` (14) also returns a decrypted L0, for PreKey first messages and the held replay, so the file key would reach Kotlin or the media message would be lost | yes: `jni.rs:1268-1269` returns `Field::L0`; `HeldReplay.kt:27` and `DmSessions.kt:510` call `sessionInbound`; F9 §2.6 row 14, §6.8, §7.4 R7; revision 1 also wrongly said the held replay calls `decrypt` | fixed: function 14 wraps and zeroes like 13; lists, ADR-44, Q10 amended; tests through 14 and the held replay; mutant PM-M17; first-message media in the worlds and SCN-MC | D7, X-M9, §4.2, §13.4, §15.2, §15.3, §15.4, §15.9, ADR-44, Q10 |
| RV-C2 = RV-P6 | **High.** First binder wins: a card holder who lists `M` names a victim's fragment hashes in its own manifest, captures them during the victim's upload and makes the honest object `corrupt` | yes: `media_fragment.blob_hash` was the primary key; a card holder can list and get `M` (F9 §4.5, L7-D16) | fixed: content-addressed store with references (`media_blob`, `media_fragment(object_id, ordinal)`), ownership by AEAD, deletion only when unreferenced, return to pool on `corrupt`/`incomplete`; the oracle rewritten; hash-squatting world; mutant MC-M17 | D25, §3.4, §7.2, §13.6, §15.3, §15.4, §15.9 |
| RV-C3 | **Medium.** One long-lived `:mdec` process served every decode and strip for 30 s: an exploit stays resident, sees later images and shapes the user's next sends | yes (design text; `bindIsolatedService` with instance names exists since API 29 = minSdk) | fixed: a process instance per request, never shared; input regions read-only, output copied after teardown, regions zeroed; L9-M11 restated; PID test; mutant PM-M21; start cost measured (V-M18); fallback Q25 | D17, §9.2, §10.3, L9-M11, §15.8, Q25 |
| RV-C4 = RV-F2 | **Medium.** The schema could not express §6.4: `attempt` per object while op ids and "at most two resends" are per ordinal; no op → ordinal map; the CHECK broke on large objects; retries could re-store or skip blobs | yes: `OutboundOutcome` names only an op id and a namespace; F9 solved it with `dm_outbound_op` (`Schema.kt:1017`) | fixed: per-ordinal `upload_state`, `media_outbound_op`, `retry_gen` and `resend_count` in the op id, READY defined over ordinals; SCN-MB per ordinal on a class-10 object; mutant MC-M18 | D13, §6.4, §13.5, §13.6, §15.3 |
| RV-C5 = RV-P4 | **Medium.** View-once (and other) key erasure was only logical: no WAL checkpoint after `key_env = NULL` | yes: F9 §10.7 checkpoints only after SWEEP | fixed: F9's TRUNCATE checkpoint after every transaction that nulls or deletes a `key_env`, before the view-once handle is returned; raw `.db`/`-wal` scans right after; mutant MC-M19 | D20, P-M8, §11.1, §11.2, §15.3, §15.5, L9-M10 |
| RV-C6 | **Low-medium.** `key_env` bound only to `object_id`, so "per destination" rested on Kotlin | yes: F9 wraps bind the contact (`wrap.rs` `olm_session_binding`) | fixed: binding = role ‖ destination ‖ `object_id` ‖ `manifest_hash`; `encrypt` refuses a mismatch; mutant PM-M22 | D7, §4.2, §13.2, §13.6 |
| RV-C7 | **Low-medium.** T35 does not apply "unchanged": `K_p`/`K_m` seal relay bytes and `K_f` rides the intro inner layer, which T35's provenance does not cover | yes: INV T35 row ("nu acoperă stratul interior intro") | fixed: T35 extended, labels specified, the gap declared for media; F-M7 | §3.8, X-M12, §14.2, F-M7, B.2 |
| RV-C8 | **Low.** Format strictness: (a) image ⇒ class 0 not enforced; (b) dimensions and duration trusted from Kotlin, bound 4 096; (c) preview pad and size not checked; (d) caption `body_env` CHECK too wide; (e) no `verified → corrupt` | yes, each in the text or schema | fixed: (a) R-D3, manifest rule, schema CHECK; (b) walker-derived, bounds 2 048 / 1 280 / `VIDEO_MAX_MS`; (c) pad zero, ≤ 320 px; (d) ≤ 28 + 1 024 B; (e) transition, trigger, mutant MC-M22; also MC-M21 | §3.5, §4.1, §7.3, §7.6, §8.2, §13.2, §13.6 |
| RV-C9 | **Low.** AAC is outside the canonical fMP4 profile (data and fill elements carry bytes) | yes: the walker checked only AVC NAL types | declared (parsing AAC elements needs a decoder); forced audio re-encoding makes it irrelevant for the honest path; V-M12 covers encoders' AAC elements; Q35 | §8.3, P-M6, T43, Q35 |
| RV-C10 | **Low.** Claim accuracy: (a) CVE severities; (b) why the tag is uniform; (c) no multi-user gain from the salt; (d) commitment gives no sender attribution; (e) the seeded `getrandom` backend does not exist, and must not rewind if built | (a) yes: the 2026-09 bulletin lists CVE-2026-28609 as High (Framework) and CVE-2026-28618 as Critical (System, media codecs); (b)–(e) yes | corrected in place | §9.1, §3.3 R-O1, X-M3, §3.2, §3.4, §3.7, §15.1 |
| RV-C11 | **Low.** The view-once sender cleanup ignored a final INDETERMINATE descriptor | yes | fixed: cleanup after any final outcome other than FAILED; a FAILED one at the deadline; mutant MC-M20 | §6.4, §11.2, ADR-47 |
| RV-P1 | **High.** Transformer silently transmuxes a source that already matches the output, so a phone video could arrive as the camera's own bitstream | yes: Media3 documentation ("automatically switches to transmuxing"); `TransformerUtil.shouldTranscodeVideo/Audio`; `Codec.EncoderFactory.videoNeedsEncoding()`/`audioNeedsEncoding()` default `false`; `ExportResult.*EncoderName` "null if none were used" | fixed: forced encoding, encoder-name check, HDR tone-mapping, matching-source corpus row, V-M17, mutant PM-M18 | D15, §6.2, §8.3, §15.7, T43 |
| RV-P2 = RV-F8 | **High.** At `POOL_CAP` ingestion stopped, then fetching stopped; since binding frees pool space, fetching depended on descriptor state (a reflex); and the flood bound was understated (distinct blobs per relay; a card holder's own write capabilities) | yes: F7 `FETCHED_CAP`; F9 §4.5 ("write grants read"), L7-D16 | fixed: eviction, never a stop; a free-space floor that discards; NI-M6 and PM-M19; the bound restated with Phase 12's rule as the closure | D10, P-M4, §5.3, §7.1, §7.5, §12.2, L9-M3, L9-M4, Q24 |
| RV-P3 | **High.** A burst truncated by the expiry of a 5-minute message separated 5-minute from 1-hour timers (same bucket), and JM1's "exactly F + 1" could not hold for FAILED or deadline-bound objects | yes: R-T1 merges the two timers so relays cannot tell them apart; the countdown ran from `MEDIA-COMMIT` | fixed: the sender countdown starts at the upload's end (bounded by `deadline + timer`, an R-T6 "only earlier" update); JM1's exemptions stated; the partial burst declared in `LM` and L9-M13; NI-M7 and PM-M20; EP-M made consistent with frozen outbox payloads | D26, P-M1, §2.2, §6.3, §6.4, §11.1, §15.5, §15.6, Q26 |

| RV-F4 | **High.** Owner-only actions block the critical path and were not called out (S0's phone, relay and pin; the skipped `android` job; the KVM runner) | yes: STATUS; the Phase 9 memory of owner actions | fixed: S0 split into S0a (gates S1) and S0b (gates only the checklist); local runs as slice evidence; Q31, Q32; R-M15 | D23, §15.1, §15.10, §17.1, §17.3 |
