| ID | Finding (short) | Verified | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RV-C11 (tail), RV-P6 (tail) | The rest of crypto L6 (clean up at a final INDETERMINATE too) and of privacy M3 (bind by `(object_id, ordinal)`, reference-counted files, a world case and a mutant) | read in full | **already handled** in revision 2 (MC-M20; D25, MC-M17); nothing to add | §6.4, §7.2 |
| RV-P7 | **Medium.** Automatic download plants decryptable media from strangers: a descriptor in a request creates an object with its key, openable for up to the request's 7 days on a seized device | yes: F9 §12.3 stores a request's content rows like any contact's (`request_until_day = today + 7`); revision 2's §7.2 created a `media_object` with `key_env` for every kind-5 L0 | **fixed:** no object and no key for a request (downloaded, never openable, not restored on accept); P-M9; the request world case, EP-M paths, seizure check, SCN-MC; mutant PM-M24; owner question **Q36** (default: keep no key); F-M10 for a request's text | D28, P-M9, §2.1, §6.5, §7.2, §13.4, §15.2–§15.6, ADR-45, Q36 |
| RV-P8 | **Medium.** A timer change during the upload splits the object's TTL: fragments keep the commit's bucket, the descriptor gets `ttlFor` of the new timer | yes: F9 §7.3 S8 computes `ttlFor(conv, kind)` in `SEND`, which `MEDIA-READY` ran after the upload; LIMITE L5's pending TIMER_SET defect has the same shape | **fixed:** R-MT4 (the object's bucket, deadline and timer govern its descriptor); JM4, SCN-MB, EP-M (5), the timer-change world case; mutant PM-M25 | D12, X-M9, §5.4, §6.4, §13.4, §15, ADR-45 |
| RV-P9 | **Medium.** Stream parameters are not canonicalised: exact image dimensions (a screenshot names the screen), frame rate, audio sample rate and channels, HDR | yes, and worse than stated: revision 2 claimed a `Presentation` capped 30 fps, but `Presentation` has no frame-rate control (its source, 2026-09-25); `FrameDropEffect`, `SonicAudioProcessor.setOutputSampleRateHz` and `ChannelMixingAudioProcessor` exist. HDR was already tone-mapped (RV-P1). Partly: ADR-08's "fără locale/model/OS" is about protocol messages and device atoms, so this extends ADR-08's intent rather than breaking its letter | **fixed:** canonical geometry (image ladder, 1 280 × 720 video with bars), ≤ 30 fps by frame dropping, 48 kHz stereo AAC, enforced by the walkers; the corpus row; mutant PM-M26; V-M24; owner question **Q37** (default: canonicalise); what survives declared (aspect ratio, a frame rate below 30) | D29, P-M6, §3.5, §8.2, §8.3, §8.5, T43, L9-M9, R-M18, Q6, Q7, Q37 |

### 21.3 S0 corrections (revision 4, 2026-10-04)

Slice S0 ran the V-M checks of Appendix C.3 on the API 29 and API 35 emulators (`docs/design/faza11-s0-evidence.md`, the spike `ghost/spikes/media-s0`, evidence tooling only). Two adversarial reviews of that evidence (honesty and reproducibility, H1–H15; design impact and completeness, I1–I19; neither text was cut) were verified finding by finding, every challenged measurement was re-run, and the evidence document's Appendix W gives each finding's verdict (none rejected outright; I1's B-frame and I2's image sub-claims accepted only in part). The corrections below are folded into the normative sections; this list says where each landed.

## 22. Implementation notes

Notes recorded by the implementation slices, each in its own subsection (22.1 is the S0a remainder on API 37, 22.2 is S1, 22.3 is S2, 22.4 is S2's JVM verification, 22.5 is S3; 22.6 is reserved for S4).

### 22.1 S0a remainder: API 37 (2026-10-04)

Q47 was answered yes, and S0a's emulator-dependent checks were run on an API 37 emulator exactly as on API 29 and 35: the Google APIs image `google/sdk_gphone64_x86_64/emu64xa:17/CE2A.260420.019` (Android 17, userdebug, kernel 6.12), emulator 37.1.11, software GL; reports in `ghost/spikes/media-s0/results/api37/`, line-by-line comparison in `docs/design/faza11-s0-evidence.md`, Appendix A37. **S1 is not affected.** In all 49 Transformer outputs API 37 has API 35's box inventory, SPS, `colr`, rotations, key-frame spacing and fragments; its seven BT.601 goldens pass the corrected profile walker (`mediatool profile`) and have the structure of the committed goldens; its seven canonical STRIP images are byte-identical to the committed API 35 goldens; the colour-by-content and rate-control numbers equal API 35's to the digit; the isolated-process mechanism (V-M18, V-M19, MC-M23) and the crash and picker residues are the same in kind. Two S1 test notes only: an `APPn` can stand between `DHT` and `SOS` (the Ultra HDR MPF segment, S0-30), and the AAC track can be 0.11 s longer than the video (S0-31). V-M22 gives API 35's figures (160 s: CBR +0.3 % smooth and grain, +164 % mixed, VBR +3 % to +29 %; the 15-fps retry numbers equal; no stall in 9 long exports), and one DESIGN-mode noise export died of an `OutOfMemoryError` for want of the fragment bound (S0-2) and the key-frame pin (S0-22). R-M20 is closed; what stays open is the image type (Google APIs only; no AOSP "default" image for API 37 was measured) and S0b. The run found nine findings (S0-25 … S0-33), one of them with an owner question (Q-S11); none reopens a decision:

| Finding | What API 37 showed | Lands in |
|---|---|---|
| S0-25 | the prepared AVD's `.ini` says `target=android-0`: the guest then runs OpenGL ES 2.0 and SurfaceFlinger aborts every 12–20 s; `target=android-37` fixes it with the README's boot line | §16.2 boot check (S9) |
| S0-26 | the default AVC decoder of the image (`c2.goldfish.h264.decoder`) cannot be fed by an app (SELinux denies its fast message queue): 22 of 24 exports fail with `DECODING_FAILED`; the software decoder works; no retry exists | §6.2 one retry with a software decoder (S5; owner question Q-S11, default: retry once); `-feature -HardwareDecoder` in the job's boot (S9) |
| S0-27 | HEIC and AVIF abort the isolated process (Codec2Client check); the platform now parses them in the caller (CrabbyAvif) | §9.2 unknowns, Q39 (b) and R-M3 wording |
| S0-28 | task snapshots live in a per-user UUID directory; protected ones are 128 × 227 placeholders | §10.3, §15.8 (recursive scan) |
| S0-29 | the photo picker of the image is a separate app with its own cache and a "Done" tap; residues as API 35's | L9-M15, §15.8, S9 driver |
| S0-30, S0-31 | MPF after DHT in Ultra HDR JPEGs; the AAC track up to 0.11 s longer | S1 vectors and walker tolerance |
| S0-32, S0-33 | weaker inode adjacency and a 3 000-line root listing on the busy Google image | §12.4 (L9-M6), §15.8 item 1 |

### 22.2 Forward notes (S1 implementation, 2026-10-04)

**Precedence.** These notes record slice S1: the pure Rust crate `ghost-client-media` (`client-core/media`), its vector files, the JDK replay, its tests and mutants, and gate G-M7. They win over §0 … §21 for the items they name, and for nothing else. **No wire format, derivation, label or rule of §3, §4.1, §8.2 or §8.3 changed**: every value of the S0 spike's format that S1 recomputed agrees with this design's text, and the walker accepts every committed golden byte for byte. Where the walker states a rule the design text does not (row 9), it is S1's reading of "anything else is `NON_CANONICAL`" (§8.3), each rule accepting all 28 goldens and each an S0b checkpoint for the device goldens.

**Read first.** (1) The crate seals and reads every golden back to itself, and the `mfra` it builds from a golden's fragments equals the golden's own byte for byte (14 of 14): the sealer appends exactly the `mfra` layout whose seeking S0 validated (V-M2, `tfdt` inserted, entries from the video `tfdt`s). (2) The `aead-stream` 0.6.0 differential oracle of §15.2 item 1, §16.3 and ADR-44 point 10 is in the dev graph (row 15): S1's first version had left it out under a restriction of the orchestration brief, which the approved ADR-44 does not make, and the review of S1 put it back (row 27 records that review, row 28 what it leaves to S2). (3) The JDK replay lives where §3.9 names it, `android/media/src/test`, not in `:crypto-bridge`, and replays four of the five vector files; the fMP4 file is the Rust walker's (row 13).

| # | Kind | Section | What was built, decided or found, and why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | built | §3, §13.1 | **The crate** `ghost-client-media` (`rlib`, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, `#![deny(missing_docs)]`; outside tests `clippy::{unwrap_used, expect_used, panic, unreachable, indexing_slicing, arithmetic_side_effects, cast_possible_truncation}` denied, so no index, unchecked sum or `unwrap` can panic on untrusted bytes; one `expect` remains, on HKDF's fixed 32-byte output, `keys.rs`). No randomness: `K_f` and `object_id` are inputs (§3.7 point 3). |
| 2 | built | §3.7, CS-M1 | **Sealer.** `begin(K_f, object_id, kind, view_once, max_class)`, `append`, `finish(preview)`, `abort`. Every appended byte is walked before it is sealed (D16), so **no byte the walker refused is ever sealed** (a refusal in a later append leaves the chunks sealed before it, which hold accepted bytes only, and spends the sealer; S1's first wording, "a non-canonical stream never yields a chunk", was wrong for that case: the review of S1; test `the_sealer_never_seals_a_refused_byte`); the one-chunk lookahead holds at most `CHUNK_PT` plaintext bytes, in a buffer that never grows in place (row 21); a 256-bit used-index set refuses a second seal of an index (`INDEX_REUSED`); `finish` completes the walk, appends a video's `mfra`, computes the class from `content_len`, seals the held chunk and the zero chunks with the last flag on `n − 1` only, and builds the manifest from the **walked** width, height and duration (the caller supplies none, MC-M21). Any refusal spends the sealer (`SEQUENCE` after it); `OVERSIZE` above `max_class` (or `IMAGE_MAX_CONTENT` for an image, whatever class is asked), checked on every append and again after the `mfra`. |
| 3 | built | §3.4, §7.3, §13.2 M5–M8, CS-M2 | **Reader.** `open(K_f, Expected, blob)` checks, in this order, the blob's hash against the descriptor's (`HASH_MISMATCH`), the commitment (`COMMIT_MISMATCH`), the tag (`NOT_OPEN`), every §3.5 rule including the preview's canonical JPEG and preview geometry (`NON_CANONICAL`), and the descriptor's `object_id`, kind, flag and class (`NON_CANONICAL`). `validate_chunk` takes every index **in order** (the walker is streaming) and `validate_finish` compares the walked width, height and duration with the manifest's; `read_chunk` is random access and writes its out buffer only when index, length, fragment hashes, tag with the last flag and zero pad all passed (every refusal leaves the buffer untouched: the corruption matrix zeroes it first and asserts it stays zero). **Read mode does not walk and does not ask for a validation pass** (§7.4 opens a new reader for every display of a verified object, and reads the sender's own objects without one), so an object whose validation failed still decrypts in read mode: the gate before exposure is S2's (row 28). |
| 4 | built | §3.5, §4.1 | **Codecs.** The manifest plaintext (65 488 B) encodes only what `validate` accepts and decodes strictly (zero preview pad, zero tail, `F` pairwise distinct hashes, `class(content_len) = class`, the geometry of D29, image ⇒ class 0 and duration 0, view-once ⇒ no preview); the blob is `commit ‖ AEAD(K_m, 0¹², AD_m, MP)`. The descriptor (86 + caption ≤ 1 024 B) decodes exactly (R-D3: version, kind, flag, class, image ⇒ class 0, caption length and strict UTF-8, no trailing byte, at most 1 110 B) and carries the key slot as it is (zero-slot enforcement is S2's `l0Decode`). |
| 5 | built | §8.2, §13.2 M9 | **JPEG.** One streaming walker in two modes: `check` (canonical: an `APPn` or `COM` is refused, so `canonicalize(x) = x` exactly when `check(x)` passes) and `canonicalize` (drops `APP0`–`APP15` and `COM`, keeps every other byte, refuses the rest). It reports `SOF0`'s geometry; the D29 checks are the sealer's (image ladder) and the manifest's (preview). Memory: one segment (≤ 65 533 B), sized once and wiped. **Bounded tables (the review of S1):** each quantisation table `Tq`, each Huffman table `(Tc, Th)` and the `DRI` at most once, each `SOF0` component coded by exactly one scan (so at most three scans), and every table a scan uses defined before it, so the bytes outside the entropy-coded data are bounded (about 1.4 KiB) where S1's first walker took 16 000 `DQT` segments (1.1 MB) as canonical; all 14 JPEG goldens (one scan, `DQT` 0 and 1, four `DHT`) and the tests' builders pass. `jpegCanonicalize` returns the picture in a wiped buffer sized once to its input, wiped too when it refuses (row 21). |

| 9 | decision | §8.3 "anything else is `NON_CANONICAL`" | **Walker rules the design text does not spell out**, each accepting all 14 fMP4 goldens, each to be re-checked against the S0b device goldens before video is enabled on a phone class (S0-22): (a) a video sample is a sync sample **exactly** when it holds an IDR slice, and the `trun` sample flags are exactly `0x02000000` (sync) or `0x01010000` (non-sync) for video and `0x02000000` for audio; (b) every audio sample lasts 1 024 ticks and holds 1 … 1 536 bytes (one AAC-LC stereo access unit); (c) the trafs' samples tile the `mdat` in `traf` order with no gap or overlap; (d) an in-band SPS or PPS is byte-identical to the `avcC`'s (a device whose encoder repeats parameter sets with other VUI bytes would fail: the S0b goldens decide whether to relax this to "walked like the `avcC`'s"); (e) a VUI sample aspect ratio, when present, is square (idc 0, 1, or 255 with equal sides), like `pasp`; (f) a High-profile `avcC` may carry the ISO/IEC 14496-15 extension (`FD F8 F8 00`) or none (no High golden exists to say which Media3 writes); (g) bounds: `moov` ≤ 64 KiB, `moof` ≤ 256 KiB, ≤ 1 024 fragments, an SEI NAL unit ≤ 1 KiB **header included** (S1's first bound was on the payload, 1 025 bytes in all: the review of S1), parameter sets ≤ 512 B, a video sample ≥ 5 B, no `largesize` or to-end box; (h) the coded frame of row 6 (80 × 45 macroblocks, no cropping) and level 3.1's picture-buffer bounds — an S0b checkpoint like the others: a hardware encoder that codes 720 lines as 736 with a 16-line crop would be refused, and its goldens would decide whether to allow exactly that crop (80 × 46, bottom 16), never "any frame that crops to 1 280 × 720"; (i) the H.264 header bits and the boxes' pinned values: SPS and PPS NAL headers with the forbidden bit clear and a non-zero `nal_ref_idc` (types 7 and 8), the `avcC`'s profile, compatibility and level bytes equal to the SPS's, `mdhd` timescales pinned per track type (90 000 video, 48 000 audio). JPEG (§8.2's table does not list them): no fill bytes, restart markers only with a non-zero `DRI` and in cyclic order, baseline table limits (DHT class and number ≤ 1; scan selectors ≤ 1; `Ss` 0, `Se` 63, `Ah` = `Al` = 0), the well-formedness of each segment (`SOF0` 8-bit with 1 or 3 distinct components sampled 1 … 4 with `Tq` 0 … 3; `SOS` naming 1 … `Nf` distinct `SOF0` components; `DHT` tables of 1 … 256 codes; `DQT` 8-bit tables, `Tq` 0 … 3), and the bounded tables of row 5. Sniffer: 32 image brands refused — `mif1 mif2 mif3 msf1 miaf 1pic heic heix heim heis hevc hevx hevm hevs avci avcs jpeg jpgs vvic vvis evbi evbs j2ki j2is avif avis avio MA1A MA1B MiHA MiHB MiHE` (S1's first 16 and those the review of S1 found missing) — and an `ftyp` the 64 bytes do not hold whole is `UNSUPPORTED`. **Bytes the profile leaves free (declared; the review of S1):** besides slice data and the AAC frames (Q35, P-M6), the PPS after its header (≤ 512 B; its in-band copies byte-identical), the bodies of SEI messages 0, 1 and 6 (≤ 1 KiB per NAL unit, any number of units within a sample and the 16-MiB fragment), the SPS values the walk reads without bounding (timing, HRD), the `esds` buffer size and bitrates (11 B), and the JPEG table values (bounded by row 5). Each is decoder syntax, not a metadata field (the walker refuses the user-data SEI, payload types 4 and 5, where encoders write version strings, §8.3 point 3, and every metadata box), so a modified sender gains no channel wider than the slice data it controls anyway, and an honest encoder's values are device-class bits (§8.5). P-M6 and Q35 name only the AAC case: S10 folds this list into P-M6 (Appendix B). Options if S9 or the owner wants them narrower: walk the PPS in full (FMO and redundant pictures refused), bound the SEI units per sample, pin the `esds` bitrate fields to the requested 128 kbit/s — each to be checked against the S0b device goldens first. |

| 21 | finding | §2.5 practice, D7 | **Key copies inside the AEAD and HKDF instances are not wiped.** `chacha20poly1305` 0.11.0 zeroises its key on drop only with its `zeroize` feature, which no crate of the shipped graph enables (there it has `alloc`, `default` and `getrandom`, the last two through vodozemac's `hpke`), and `hkdf` 0.13.0 keeps its PRK in a plain struct; so each cipher or HKDF instance leaves a copy of `K_p`, `K_m` or the PRK in freed memory, in this crate as in `ghost-client-dm` and `ghost-client-mls`. Enabling `chacha20poly1305/zeroize` is a G-R2-visible feature change of the shipped graph: an owner decision, best taken with S2's link. **What the crate itself wipes (corrected by the review of S1):** S1's first version claimed "the crate's own secrets and plaintext buffers are zeroised" while the sealer's held chunk, emptied with `mem::take` and regrown by the next append, freed the whole last partial chunk of every video whose length is not a multiple of 262 128 unwiped during `finish` (the review measured a 213 549-byte block with an allocator that scans freed blocks), `jpegCanonicalize` returned the picture in a plain `Vec` and dropped its partial output unwiped on a refusal, and `Descriptor::encode` returned the body, whose slot carries `K_f` inside S2's `encrypt`, in a plain `Vec`. Now every buffer the crate owns that holds key material or content is wiped when freed and never grown in place (sized once, or grown by moving it and wiping the old block, `bytes::extend_wiped`): `K_f`, `K_p`, `K_m`, the held chunk, decrypted chunks, manifest plaintexts and previews, the walkers' segment, box, SEI and parameter-set buffers, the canonical JPEG and the descriptor body. `tests/zeroize.rs` scans every block freed while sealing (whole chunks then the remainder, and pieces of 100 003, 997, 4 096 and 7 bytes), reading, canonicalising (accepted and refused) and encoding, for canaries planted in slice data, entropy-coded data, a quantisation table, the PPS, an SEI payload, the preview and the key slot, and finds none; mutants ms27 … ms29 put the three leaks back and are detected. **Not wiped by the crate:** the caller's buffers (its input slices and the reader's `out`), the ciphertext it returns, the walked structure (offsets and times, the `mfra`), a decoded descriptor's caption (message text, held like F9's `DmContent` body), and the key copies above. So §10.5 and §15.2 item 8 hold for the crate's own native buffers; the JVM half is S7's. No `Debug` of the crate renders a key, a caption, a preview or a walker buffer (S1's first `Descriptor`, `Manifest` and `SealedObject` printed the caption and the preview; the DM crate's `DmContent` rule now applies; mutants ms51, ms52). |

### 22.3 Forward notes (S2 implementation, 2026-10-13)

**Built (slice S2, all uncommitted on `p11-s2` at the time of writing).** (1) The closed `MediaNative` surface of §13.2 lives in `ghost-client-dm`'s `jni_media.rs` (behind `jni-bridge`, the same DM context and its registries, §13.1): M1–M10 exactly as the table names them, the two host-only entries `testVectors` and `testSealBeginWithKey` (§15.6's model-relay re-derivation, `test-vectors` feature, the `DmEquivalence` precedent), the reply codec `code(1) || TLV fields`, the `MediaCode` enumeration (14 codes; `SEQUENCE` per §22.2 row 23), the `MEDIA_FUNCTION_CODES` table and the `export_media_jni!` / `export_media_test_jni!` macros that `ghost-client-net` and `ghost-dm-host` invoke. Sealer/reader registries sit in the DM context (2 sealers, 8 readers) and `ctxClose` drops and zeroises them with it. (2) The key slot of §4.2: `encrypt` (§2.6 function 8) takes `mediaKeyEnv`, opens it under the `(out, contact, object_id, manifest_hash)` binding and fills the slot with `K_f`; `decrypt` (13) and `sessionInbound` (14, the first message **and** the held replay) read the slot out, wrap it into a fresh `key_env` under the `in` binding and re-zero the slot before the L0 reaches Kotlin — the codec's `media_slot_fill` / `media_slot_take`, the L0 in the database always has a zero slot. (3) The `MediaFileKey` wrap name and `media_file_key_binding` in `wrap.rs`. (4) The `M` labels in functions 2–4. (5) The S2 link: `ghost-client-dm` names `ghost-client-media` a normal dependency, no feature but `default`; the crate's dev graph carries the cycle back (the `[key_env]` section of `media_object.txt` is generated through the shipped wrap code, §3.9 row 12 done); G-R2's shipped-graph clause and the G-R8 scan's feature source turn themselves on at this link (§22.2 row 19's tripwire). (6) The Kotlin sources of `:crypto-bridge`: `MediaNative` (the only class declaring media natives, internal + `@JvmName`), `MediaResult` and the typed facade `MediaCrypto`; `DmNative`/`DmResult`/`DmSessions` carry `mediaKeyEnv` and the held-replay kind-5 rule. (7) `ghost-dm-host` exports the media list and its two test entries. (8) The G-M5 gate: `dm-jni-surface.sh` extended (the exported `Java_org_ghost_crypto_bridge_MediaNative_*` symbols equal §13.2's list; `testVectors`/`testSealBeginWithKey` only in host and test libraries).

**Verified on this tree (cargo-only, 2026-10-13).** `cargo build -p ghost-dm-host --release --locked --features provenance`: green. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`: clean. `cargo clippy --locked -p ghost-client-media -p ghost-client-dm -p ghost-dm-host --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo test -p ghost-client-media -p ghost-client-dm --locked`: **300 passed, 0 failed** (media 67, dm 233 — dm's lib tests include `jni_media`'s wire/closed-list cases and the key-slot round trips; media's 67 include the `vectors` cases that read the new `[key_env]` section).

**Recorded deviations and out-of-slice fixes.** (a) M2 and M6 cross byte arrays, not direct `ByteBuffer`s: the shipped graph forbids `unsafe_code` and JNI's `GetDirectBufferAddress` cannot be read without an `unsafe` seam — safe and correct, one copy where the design wanted zero-copy; the direct-buffer form is a pending owner decision (a documented, capacity-checked `unsafe` helper in a crate that may contain it). (b) `ghost-client-mls`'s `jni.rs` used `chunks_exact(48)` in `join_verify`'s invite parsing; the toolchain's lint `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` (1.98.1, warn-by-default, `-D warnings` in `rust-gates.sh`) flags it, so the whole-workspace clippy gate could not pass on any branch — fixed mechanically (`as_chunks::<48>()`), no behaviour change, one line outside S2's files.

(2) ~~The G-R6 lists' gate run~~ **done 2026-10-13** (§22.4; the shipped `rust` job's remaining gates are CI-side, unchanged). ### 22.4 Forward notes (S2's JVM verification through the host build, 2026-10-13)

The exit criteria §22.3 deferred while the 64-seed sweep held the toolchain, run the evening the sweep ended, all uncommitted on `p11-s2`. Host library: `ghost/target/release/ghost_dm_host.dll` (release, `--locked --features provenance`), rebuilt once during the session for the finding below.

**The suites, through `-Dghost.dm.native` on the host build.** `:crypto-bridge:testDebugUnitTest`, **116 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped** (nothing assumed away — the library loads and the native suites ran in full):

- `MediaResultCodesTest` (**10**): the 14 `MediaCode`s and the 19 shipped `MediaField` tags against `jni_media.rs` byte for byte and name for name (the four `testVectors`-only tags pinned by name); every facade function's `when` arms exactly `MEDIA_FUNCTION_CODES` + `OK` + `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE`; wire round-trips, undefined bytes, reply erasure, malformed-reply strictness.
- `MediaSealBeginWithKeyHostTest` (**7**, the function-14 test): `testVectors`' derivations reproduce the vector file's `k_p`/`k_m`/`commit`; `op_id` is 16 bytes, deterministic, resend-separating; the `key_env` oracle reproduces `[key_env]`'s `out`/`in` envelopes; and a full re-seal of `[object image_class0]` through `testSealBeginWithKey → sealAppend → sealFinish` reproduces the committed `manifest_hash` and all 16 fragment hashes byte for byte, with the registry cap (`LIMIT`) and the sealer state machine (`NON_CANONICAL` spends, then `SEQUENCE`; a finished sealer's id is foreign → `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE`) and the malformed-request refusals.
- `MediaHeldReplayHostTest` (**4**, the held replay through the facade): a kind-5 L0 (built through the shipped `l0Encode`; `DmContent.Kind` deliberately refuses MEDIA until S3) as a first flight through `sessionInbound` (function 14, the held-replay driver) and on the settled session through `decrypt` (function 13) — both land `key_env` (60 bytes) bound `(in, contact, object_id, manifest_hash)`, return the L0 **byte-identical with a zero slot**, and the taken envelope opens a reader on the very manifest `sealFinish` produced: the validation pass walks all 4 chunks, read mode returns the walked content with the zero padding trimmed, read without the readable attestation is `SEQUENCE` (§22.2 row 28a), an out-of-range index is `INDEX_RANGE`, and a media L0 without its envelope (or a text L0 with one) is refused as the caller bug it is.
- Pre-existing suites all green after the fallout fixes named below.

**The gates.** `dm-jni-surface.sh` (G-R6 + G-M5, source halves; the symbol half is Windows-skipped by design) **OK**; the source-only `ch-derivation-inputs.sh`, `dm-derivation-inputs.sh`, `sync-no-catch-all.sh` and `kotlin-clearnet.sh` (the last needs a UTF-8 `LC_ALL` under Git Bash) all **OK**. The dll's exports were read manually with the NDK's `llvm-readobj --coff-exports` (a release MSVC binary has no COFF symbol table for `nm`): exactly `DmNative` 29 = the `Exported` closed list, `MediaNative` 12 (§13.2's ten + the two host-only test entries), `DmEquivalence` 4, `MlsNative` 27, `MlsVectors` 1, and no JNI symbol under any other class — the same check the gate's ELF half automates.

**The channel sanity.** `:messaging:testDebugUnitTest --tests ChannelJoinTest`: **12 tests, 0 failures** — the dm changes did not regress the engine path.

**What the verification caught (each fixed in the working tree, nothing committed).** (a) **T45 violation in the committed JNI layer**: `decrypt` and `sessionInbound` built the reply's `L0` field **before** `media_slot_take` re-zeroed the slot — `Reply::field` copies eagerly, so `K_f` reached Kotlin inside the returned L0 (the host test saw `k_f[1]` at element 55; the cargo-only round trips could not see it, exactly why this verification was the exit criterion). Fixed by taking the slot first in both functions; the host library was rebuilt and every assertion above re-run green. (b) **Test-only fallout of the strict slot decode**: S2's `encrypt` validates the L0 with the codec, and five pre-S2 crypto-bridge tests handed it raw byte payloads — they now encode text L0s through the facade (`DmSessionsNativeTest`, `DmRevocationNativeTest`), as the send pipeline does. (c) **`MediaNative` must declare its two host-only entries**: §13.2 names them `MediaNative.testVectors`/`testSealBeginWithKey` and the JNI symbol pins the declaring class, so unlike `DmEquivalence`'s they cannot live on a test-only class — the declarations sit in the production file (inert on a device: a shipped library exports no such symbol, G-M5's library half checks precisely that), and `dm-jni-surface.sh`'s media Kotlin half gained the same `^test` carve-out its Rust half already had. (d) `sync-no-catch-all.allow` gained the two S2 files' deliberate `UnsatisfiedLinkError` catches (the `DmNative`/`DmFacade` rule, one each). (e) `DmResultCodesTest`'s whole-package arm scan now scopes itself to `DmCode` blocks, since `MediaCrypto`'s media blocks share the package.

**The review round (F1–F3, native hygiene; fixed in the working tree the same day, nothing committed).** An S2 review found three places a media key outlived its welcome in native memory; each is fixed and re-verified through the host build.

- **F1 — `readerOpen`'s unwrapped `K_f` copy** (`jni_media.rs`): the unwrap's `plain` is a `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`, but the conversion `let key_f = <[u8; 32]>::try_from(plain.as_slice())` copied `K_f` into a plain `[u8; 32]` local that was then handed (by copy) to `media::Reader::open` and never erased. Fixed with the codec's own pattern (`codec.rs`'s `media_slot_fill` half): the result of `Reader::open` is bound, `key_f.zeroize()` runs, and only then the `?` — so the local is erased on the refusal paths too.
- **F2 — `out.plaintext` on the error paths of `decrypt` and `sessionInbound`** (`jni.rs`): the decrypted L0's slot still holds `K_f` until `media_slot_take` re-zeros it, and three exits drop the buffer before that happens — the take's own error returns (slot still filled) and the state re-wrap failure above it (`let new_state = new_state?` / `let state_env = state_env?`), the codebase's own rule at the unwrapped-states loop (§2.5: a plaintext goes no further than this call). Fixed by moving the buffer out into `let mut plaintext = Zeroizing::new(std::mem::take(&mut out.plaintext))` right after the session call succeeds: every exit path — success, re-wrap refusal, take refusal — now erases it, and the `L0` field is built from the same wrap after the take.
- **F3 — the sealer/reader registries held entries by value** (`jni.rs`'s `DmContext` + `jni_media.rs`): `HashMap::remove`'s bit-for-bit move leaves the vacated bucket bytes stale — the removed entry's inline `K_f`/`K_p` stay readable until the table is rehashed or freed (a growing table frees its old allocation unerased) — the exact residue class CRY-P9-02 fixed at the context level, contradicting the doc comments. Fixed the same way the context was boxed: the registries now store `Box<SealerEntry>`/`Box<ReaderEntry>`, so a bucket holds only a pointer, removal moves no secret, and dropping the box zeroises the entry's keys in place before its allocation is freed; `SealerEntry::dest_id` (the destination identifier the `key_env` binds to) joined the wiped fields as a `Zeroizing<[u8; 16]>`. Handle/lookup semantics are untouched.

**The F3 probe** (`dm/tests/media_registry_zeroize.rs`, mirroring the media crate's `tests/zeroize.rs`: a scanning global allocator — `try_borrow`-armed, so a nested free can never panic inside the allocator — over insert/remove/rehash churn of 300 entries per registry, scanning freed blocks for 32-byte `K_f` and `K_p` patterns; the registry types and a canary-carrying entry constructor are exposed through a `#[doc(hidden)]`, `test-vectors`-gated `jni_media::probe` module, the `equivalence` precedent, so no shipped graph carries it). The pre-fix by-value shape the review probed freed **8 blocks** holding `K_f` or `K_p` (one per table growth's abandoned allocation; stable across runs, debug and release); the shipped boxed shape frees **0**. Both registry shapes are measured by the test binary: the boxed shape is asserted at 0, the by-value shape is printed as the residue demonstration.

**Re-verified after F1–F3.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo clippy --locked -p ghost-client-media -p ghost-client-dm -p ghost-dm-host --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, and the dm crate also clean with `jni-bridge`+`test-vectors` on (the probe test included). `cargo test -p ghost-client-dm --locked`: **233 passed, 0 failed**; with `jni-bridge,test-vectors` the lib's 215 (the `jni`/`jni_media` unit tests included) and the probe's 2 pass — the equivalence suite's committed-file case fails in **both** trees (reproduced with the changes stashed: `dm_equivalence.txt` does not regenerate byte-for-byte on this host under `test-vectors`, a pre-existing deviation of that feature combination, not of this round; the default-feature run the S2 numbers rest on is unaffected). Host library rebuilt (`ghost-dm-host`, release, `--locked --features provenance`) and the three media suites re-run through it on `:crypto-bridge:testDebugUnitTest --tests "org.ghost.crypto.bridge.Media*"`: **21 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped** (`MediaResultCodesTest` 10, `MediaSealBeginWithKeyHostTest` 7, `MediaHeldReplayHostTest` 4) — the held-replay assertions re-prove the F2 ordering end to end (the returned L0 is byte-identical with a zero slot on both functions).
### 22.5 Forward notes (S3 implementation, 2026-10-04)

**Precedence.** These notes record slice S3: **migration 8** (`Schema.CURRENT_VERSION` 7 → 8: the guarded drop of the two v1 media tables, the rebuild of the five DM tables that admit a media message, the five media tables with 19 triggers), the first code of `:media` — the package `org.ghost.media.store` (`MediaObjects`, `MediaFragments`, `MediaBlobs`, `CiphertextFiles`, `MediaReconcile`) —, the introspection, constraint, trigger, REPLACE-guard, residue, fault-injection and expired-row (ME-16) tests over them, SCN-MF and the storage halves of SCN-MA, SCN-MC and SCN-ME, and the scopes of the source gates that now read `android/media`. They win over §0 … §22.4 for the items they name, and for nothing else; **in particular they win over §13.6 steps 2 and 3, which name two tables to rebuild where five must be (F-S3-1, row 3).** **No Rust, wire format, derivation, vector or relay-visible observable changed, and no engine logic was written:** nothing calls the stores yet (the `MEDIA-*` transactions of §13.5 are S4 … S9), so T47 and G-M6 have nothing to read in S3 beyond the scope of their gates (rows 15 and 21). Every deviation below is an implementation choice where the design is silent, or a reading, each with its reason; every finding is evidence, with the test or the command that shows it. Written on branch `p11-s3` from `888778c` (main, 2026-10-04); rebased onto `main` (post-S2) as `p11-s3-rebase` — migration 7 lands amended (111 statements, §19.54 rows 6/12), migration 8 follows it, and this section keeps its number 22.5 (22.4 is S2's JVM verification). Row 18 is what was run, with the commit. **Revised 2026-10-05 after the adversarial review of the branch** (two lenses, rows 24 … 31): the rows above are corrected where the review showed them wrong, and rows 24 … 31 record what it found, what was fixed and what is left to the owner.

**The one thing to read first.** (1) **Migration 8 rebuilds five tables, not two** (row 3): with foreign keys on — they are, and `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is a no-op inside a transaction — `DROP TABLE dm_message` first runs an implicit `DELETE` that fires every `ON DELETE CASCADE` into `dm_outbound_item`, `dm_outbound_op`, `dm_pending_send` and `dm_reaction`; a rebuild of `dm_message` and `dm_outbound_item` alone, as §13.6 words it, would have erased the user's outbound queue and reactions at the upgrade. The migration rebuilds the whole subtree, every row copied by an explicit column list, the old tables dropped children first. (2) **M has not shipped**, so S4 … S9 may amend it where their reading of §13.5 needs a column or a rule S3 could not know: row 5 lists what S3 added beyond §13.6's trigger list, row 22 how Q27 and R-T3 would fold into the rebuild. (3) The upgrade **copies the DM tables** and so costs time and disk in proportion to them (row 17; F-S3-2, row 19): a 100 000-message database upgrades in 1.7 to 3.1 s and ends twice its size. (4) The stores are **stateless**, write only through the caller's `SyncTransaction`, use no REPLACE and no upsert, and **every read of an object carries the expiry filter**, which the lint of ME-16 runs against a world that still holds the expired rows and one that does not (row 13). (5) The ciphertext store **unlinks** on delete and never overwrites (row 11), and what an unlink leaves in the file system is measured (row 27). (6) **A sealer reserves the name of a file before it writes it** (`CiphertextFiles.newName`, `PreparingFiles`), and the temp file is named after the final name: the first version generated the name inside `write`, so the reconcile could delete the file under a sealer's hands (row 25).

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | built | §13.6 step 1 | **The guards and the drops of the v1 tables** (`migration8Guard()`, 9 statements). `v8_alter_guard`: `ALTER TABLE … RENAME` must rewrite the references of the rebuilt tables' children, which only `legacy_alter_table = OFF` does, so the migration refuses to start with the pragma on (the guard of migration 4, repeated; it is 0 only when the pragma function returns exactly one row holding 0, so a build without pragma functions fails closed; `v8RefusesToRunWithLegacyAlterTableOn`). `v8_migration_guard` with `CHECK (row_count = 0)` fed by `count(*)` of `media_blobs` and `media`, dropped again, then `DROP TABLE media_blobs; DROP TABLE media` — **children first**, so no cascade can hide a row the guard counted. A row in either table, or both, aborts the migration with the CHECK message and leaves `user_version` 7, the schema text and every row as they were (`guardAbortsV8WhenAV1MediaTableHasRows`, three cases); once the tables are emptied the same runner finishes. The two tables never had a writer; the tests that listed them count them out at v8 (`SchemaAndMigrationTest`, `Migration6Test`) or are capped at v7 (`Migration7Test`). |
| 2 | built | §13.6, CS-M7; SCN-MF | **Migration 8 as a whole:** one transaction, append-only, fail-closed; `CURRENT_VERSION` 7 → 8; **72 statements** — 9 guards and v1 drops, 34 of rebuild (5 `_v8` tables, 5 explicit-column copies, 5 drops, 5 renames, 3 indexes, 3 triggers, 5 REPLACE guards, the 3 statements of the foreign-key guard), 10 of media tables and indexes, 19 triggers — all in the region of `Schema.kt` that G-DM4 reads. `Migration8Test` (15) is SCN-MF on a **populated v7** (a row in every family v8 relies on or must leave alone: the sync tables, the entitlement tables, a channel, and the five rebuilt DM tables in every shape their checks admit — text and reaction messages, incoming and outgoing, with and without a timer, items of kinds 1, 2 and 4, item and bundle operations, a pending send, reactions from both sides): every row of every kept table is byte-identical afterwards (`quote()` of each column) and the five media tables are empty; **an injected failure at each of the 72 statements, and at the version bump, leaves the populated v7 untouched** (schema text, every row, version, no open transaction), a half-migrated database is refused by `verifyIntegrity`, and the same runner then finishes with the result of an uninterrupted run (an injected failure with rollback on `:memory:`; **the review repeated it with a real process kill** — TerminateProcess on a populated v7 file of 400 messages, at each of the 72 statements, after the version bump and after COMMIT, in WAL and rollback-journal mode: 148 kill points, 0 bad, v7 byte-identical on reopen, the rerun equal to an uninterrupted migration, a kill after COMMIT giving v8); | 3 | finding | §13.6 steps 2 and 3; F9 G-9 | **F-S3-1. The rebuild must cover `dm_message`'s whole subtree: five tables, not two.** §13.6 rebuilds `dm_message` (kind 5, the caption envelope) and `dm_outbound_item` (kind 5) and nothing else. With `foreign_keys = ON`, SQLite's `DROP TABLE` of a parent first performs an implicit `DELETE` of all its rows, and that fires the `ON DELETE CASCADE` of every child. **Evidence.** (a) `Migration8Test.droppingTheParentFirstWouldHaveCascadedAwayTheChildrenSoTheRebuildCoversTheWholeSubtree`: on the populated v7, `DROP TABLE dm_message` inside a transaction takes `dm_outbound_item` from 4 rows to 1 (the control item, which names no message), `dm_outbound_op` from 3 to 1 (the bundle operation), `dm_pending_send` from 1 to 0 and `dm_reaction` from 3 to 0. (b) The same on a bare SQLite (`node:sqlite`, 3.50.2) with one parent and a child holding 3 rows: after `BEGIN; PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF; DROP TABLE parent` the child holds 0 rows and `PRAGMA foreign_keys` still reads 1 — the pragma is a no-op inside a transaction, and the migration runs inside the open helper's transaction, so it cannot turn the keys off first; with the children copied before any drop, the rows survive (3 kept in the copy). (c) The documentation of `DROP TABLE` says the same (sqlite.org/lang_droptable.html: the implicit `DELETE` "may invoke foreign key actions"). **Consequence.** M rebuilds `dm_message`, `dm_outbound_item`, `dm_outbound_op`, `dm_pending_send` and `dm_reaction` — the four children are copied unchanged — creating the five `_v8` tables, copying all five, dropping the old ones children first, and renaming (the copy before any drop is what keeps the rows: the review dropped the parents first in a throwaway mutant and lost none, so the children-first order is the tidy one, not the safety); the foreign-key guard of migration 4 is repeated (`pragma_foreign_key_check` must be empty). §13.6's rule (a) ("copies the columns of the tables as they are at merge, generated from the current shared definitions") applies to the five. **Correction of §13.6:** made as a note at the end of its step 3, and the two steps are otherwise left as written (this note wins over them); a later revision may fold them — step 2 would read "Rebuild `dm_message` **and every table that cascades from it** (`dm_outbound_item`, `dm_outbound_op`, `dm_pending_send`, `dm_reaction`)" and step 3 would go into it. The extra cost of the three children is the copy of their rows, which are short (an operation, a pending send, a reaction); the large columns, `dm_message.body_env` and `dm_outbound_item.l0_env`, M copies in any case (row 17). |

Q27 is not decided: M copies the tables **as they are at merge** and adds nothing but the kind (row 22). |

Every comparison is NULL-safe (`IS NOT`, `COALESCE(x IN (…), 0)`, and `NEW.c IS NULL OR NEW.c < OLD.c` on the two `NOT NULL DEFAULT` counters; R-T6, and row 28 for the correction). `MediaSchemaIntrospectionTest`, `MediaSchemaConstraintsTest`, `MediaTriggersTest` and `MediaReplaceGuardsTest` (counts in row 16) pin the whole (the review ran 48 clause-level mutants of the 14 triggers and 5 guards against them: 45 killed, 1 equivalent, 2 survivors now pinned, row 31): the objects and columns exactly, every CHECK just outside and just inside its bound (at least 130 cases), the unique keys and the foreign keys again **with a second row present** (a bound held for the first row of a table is not a bound of the table), every state pair of every machine against its edge table, each guard with five forms of insert. |
| 7 | built | §10.4; S0-12 | **`CiphertextFiles`**, the only writer of media bytes. One 64 KiB file per blob under a random 16-byte name (hex on disk), written **under a name the caller reserved first** (`newName`, then `write(name, …)`; the temp file is `.t` + the hex of the final name, so one reservation covers both: row 25) as **temp → `setTimes(0, 0)` → `force` → `rename` → directory sync**, five calls and ten kill points, so a crash leaves either no file under a final name or a whole, durable one with `mtime = atime = 0`; a leftover temp file is an orphan the reconcile deletes. The `setTimes` before the force and the rename is the S0-12 fault point (`setLastModified(0)` leaves `atime`). A free-space floor (`MEDIA_FREE_FLOOR`, 256 MiB): below it an ingest gets `WriteResult.BelowFloor` and consumes the blob without a file, while a sealer writing its own object passes `enforceFloor = false` and meets a full disk as a typed `DISK_FULL` (a store directory that is not there is a typed `IO` with or without the floor, not "below the floor": row 31). Typed errors (`BAD_SIZE`, `CORRUPT_LENGTH`, `DISK_FULL`, `IO`) carry no name, path or byte, **and neither does the cause** (kept as its class, a `FileSystemException`'s reason and its frames: `RedactedIOException`; the first version kept the original, whose message names the file: row 31); the class has no network type, no coroutine, no catch-all (the one typed `IOException`), no logging and a redacted `toString`. All file operations go through the port `CiphertextFileSystem`: the real one is `NioCiphertextFileSystem` (`FileChannel` with `CREATE_NEW`, `Files.move(ATOMIC_MOVE)`), and the tests use `CrashableFileSystem`, a model that crashes before and after each call and, at the reboot, **loses what a power cut loses** — unforced data (an empty file under the final name) and unsynced directory entries (a rename that did not stick). |
| 8 | built | §10.4, §13.5 | **`MediaReconcile`**, both directions, crash-safe and idempotent. (1) Every entry of the directory that no `media_blob` row names and that is not in `preparing` is deleted and the directory synced once afterwards; (2) the times of every file it keeps are re-applied when not 0; (3) a row with no file that no fragment names (a pooled blob whose file is gone) is deleted, and one that a fragment names is **reported** (`ReconcileReport.lostNamed`, the objects affected) and not repaired — what becomes of the object (`corrupt`, `incomplete`: its key is erased, F9 §10.7's checkpoint) is the engine's, S6 and S8. **The order of the reads is the race-safety argument, and so is the order of the sealer's steps (corrected by the review, row 25):** a sealer **reserves** its name in `preparing` before any file of it exists, writes, `MEDIA-COMMIT` inserts the row and only then releases the name; the reconcile lists the directory, then takes the `preparing` snapshot, then reads the rows; so a file in the listing was reserved before the listing and left `preparing` only after its row committed (or was abandoned): it is in the snapshot or in the rows or both, and one created after the listing is not in the listing. The first version's argument was false for its own API — the name came out of `write` after the file was visible, so a sealer could not reserve it — and `MediaReconcileRaceTest` runs a reconcile at each of the ten points of a write, and two threads, with the first version's loss as its control (nine points of ten). A second run on a consistent store lists and changes nothing (`ReconcileReport.clean`, its trace is the one `list` call). |
| 9 | built | §7.2, §7.5, D25 | **`MediaBlobs`, the content-addressed store with references.** One row and one file per distinct hash, any number of `media_fragment` rows naming it, of any object: `ingest` inserts the `ingested` row and binds **every** `expected` fragment row of **any** object that names it, and returns the objects whose completeness is then checked. A blob is **pooled** when no fragment names it (an ingested one that arrived before its descriptor or that an object released); its retention day is `min(day(E) + 1, today + 8)` for `E` the relay expiry of the blob (`retentionDay`, never before day 0). `evictOverCap` deletes the pooled blobs with the earliest retention (ties by hash) **while the pool exceeds `POOL_CAP`, never stopping the claim and never fetching less** (T47); `collectExpired` is `MEDIA-GC`'s selection (`retain_until_day < today`: a blob on its last day is kept); a blob past its day that an object names is not collected and goes back to the pool with its original retention when the object is released. A sealed blob is never pooled. `deleteUnnamed` deletes only the rows nothing names, as the schema's `media_blob_referenced` requires ("a file goes only when nothing references it" is a property of the schema); `collectUnnamedSealed` deletes the sealed rows nothing names, for the deletions that bypass the store (F-S3-3, row 20; the ingested ones of such a deletion wait in the pool, row 26). A bare `insert` binds nothing; `ingest` is insert plus bind (row 5(d)). |
| 10 | built | §6.3, §7.2, §7.6, §11, §13.5 | **`MediaObjects` and `MediaFragments`.** `insertOutgoing`, `onDescriptor` (`DUPLICATE_OBJECT` writes nothing; never called for a request, D28), `insertSealed`, `insertIncoming` (`bound` if the blob row exists, whether or not another object names it, `expected` otherwise), `enqueue` (the ordinal goes `unsent` → `open` **before** the operation row is written, so a feeder defect writes no operation; ≤ 32 open per object, ≤ 64 per device; a first operation, resend 0, in the upload's current generation), `insertResend` (same ordinal and generation as the operation it repeats, `resend_count + 1`: the previous operation must exist — its generation is the ordinal's, which is not the upload's when a retry went by while the ordinal was in flight), `settle`, `allAcknowledged`, `retryUpload` (generation first, ≤ 3), the whole incoming machine (`markFetching`, `markComplete` — only with the `F + 1` rows of the object's class all bound, one guarded statement, row 31 —, `markVerified`, `markCorrupt` and `markIncomplete`, which erase the key and **release** the references — an ingested blob that nothing names returns to the pool, a sealed one is deleted —, `recordDecodeFailure`, `markUndecodable`), `openViewOnce` (the row half of `VIEW-ONCE-OPEN`: `viewing` and `key_env = NULL` in one statement) and `closeViewOnce`/`eraseSenderCopy` (**erase**: every blob no row names any more, whatever its origin), and `deleteObjectsOfMessages` (SWEEP). Transitions are guarded `UPDATE`s whose changed-row count decides: `ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` for a row not in a state the machine moves from, `MISSING_ROW` for no row, `ROW_EXISTS`, `BAD_VALUE` (the CHECKs' sizes and grids, refused first with the column named and no value), `LIMIT`. Every row type's `toString` prints states and kinds and no byte (`assertRedacted`). |
| 11 | deviation | §10.4, §11.1, Q18 | **Deleting a ciphertext file removes its name and overwrites nothing.** The brief for this slice said the deletion "overwrites per the design"; the design prescribes no overwrite of a ciphertext file. §10.4's reconcile "deletes every file that no `media_blob` row names", §11.1 relies on **crypto-shredding** (the key is erased from the database and the checkpoint removes its old pages; "a crash between a deletion's commit and the reconcile leaves ciphertext whose key is already erased"), and Q18 rejects the slot-arena file precisely because it would need in-place overwrites; an in-place overwrite promises nothing on a log-structured flash file system (§11.1: what remains below the file system "is outside the app's reach, as for every F9 deletion"). `CiphertextFiles.delete` therefore unlinks, and the claim of T44/T25 stays what §11.1 says. If the owner wants an overwrite as defence in depth it is a three-line change in `NioCiphertextFileSystem.delete` (open, write zeros, `force`, unlink) with a fault point of its own; it is not made, because it would add a write of the very bytes the rule says GHOST writes once. **What the unlink leaves in the file system is measured in row 27;** an overwrite would change none of it. |
| 12 | deviation | §10.7 point 4 (extended), §7.5, §6.4 | **A read applies the object's own deadlines as the maintenance that has not run yet would.** §10.7 point 4 prescribes the filter on `delete_at_minute`; a media row has deadlines of its own that `MEDIA-GC` and `MEDIA-DEADLINE` act on later (a frozen process, a deferred alarm), and a read in that window must not hand out what the maintenance is about to take away. The mapping is in the SQL (`CASE` in the `SELECT`, so a caller cannot forget it); the stored state is `rawState`, a bound. The lint of row 13 judges it. |

| 19 | finding | §13.6, §10.4, T33 | **F-S3-2. The rebuild copies the DM tables, so the upgrade needs up to four times the DM tables' size free, and the file stays twice as large.** Measured on the 5 000 × 20 KB case (row 17): the write-ahead log ends at 203 MiB after the commit and the main file at 203 MiB (the frames are checkpointed into it), the database before was 102 MiB — **about 406 MiB on disk at the peak for a 102 MiB database, four times** —, and after `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` the file is 203 MiB, twice: SQLite does not return the freed pages (no `VACUUM`: T33's forensic high-water mark is the same reason), later inserts reuse them. The 100 000-message case (100-byte bodies) is 33 → 61 MiB, so a typical user pays tens of MiB; a user with years of long messages pays hundreds. **Not changed in S3,** because the alternatives are worse: rebuilding only the two tables loses the children (F-S3-1); editing the CHECK text in `sqlite_master` under `writable_schema` skips the copy and is the one SQLite warns against; a column-by-column `ALTER` cannot change a CHECK. **Proposed (S10 / Phase 13, a startup matter, not a schema one):** the open path checks the free space against four times the size of the five DM tables (their pages: `dbstat` gives them) before it starts migration 8 and, below it, tells the user to free space instead of failing inside the transaction (which would roll back cleanly, but only after the copy). **Recorded for the owner,** since "the app needs 400 MiB free to update" is a user-visible fact. |
| 20 | finding → fixed | §11.1 "Deletion cascade", T25 | **F-S3-3. A contact deleted by its foreign keys left its sealed blobs and their files for ever.** §11.1 says the foreign key from `media_object.message_id` "also cascades from any other deletion of a message, such as a deleted contact; `MediaObjects` deletes the unnamed blobs in that path too". The first version of the store did that only where its own method was called (`deleteObjectsOfMessages`, SWEEP's): `DELETE FROM dm_contact` cascades through the conversation and the messages to `media_object` and its fragments, uploads and operations, and **no foreign key reaches `media_blob`** (a blob is named by hash, by design D25), so the sealed blob rows stayed, named by nothing, and each row names a file the reconcile therefore keeps — 64 KiB of dead ciphertext per fragment with no key, never collected, against T25's "no file of the object after `delete_at_minute` + lag + reconcile lag". **Found** by reading §11.1's sentence against the API (no test had deleted a contact). **Fixed in S3:** `MediaBlobs.collectUnnamedSealed(tx)` deletes the `sealed` rows no fragment names — sound at every commit boundary because `MEDIA-COMMIT` inserts a sealed blob in the transaction that inserts the fragments naming it, and a sealed blob is never pooled — and the reconcile deletes the files; an ingested blob that lost its references this way is the pool's and goes at its retention day — **a declared bound, not a safe default (row 26)**. `MediaBlobsTest.aDeletionThatBypassedTheStoreLeavesUnnamedSealedBlobsAndOnlyThoseAreCollected` (two objects, a hash shared with an incoming object, a pooled blob: 16 of 35 rows collected, the shared, the live and the pooled ones kept) and `MediaStoreScenarioTest.aDeletedContactTakesTheObjectsRowsByCascadeAndTheGcAndTheReconcileTakeTheRest` (two objects: 34 rows and 34 files gone, a second pass of each changes nothing). **For S8:** `MEDIA-GC` calls it on every pass and after such a deletion, and `deleteObjectsOfMessages` stays the way SWEEP deletes (it also erases the ingested blobs the object named). |

| 26 | finding → recorded | §11.1 (deletion cascade), T25, L9-M6 | **F-S3-5. Row 20 closed the sealed half (`collectUnnamedSealed`); the ingested half cannot be closed from the store: once the cascade took the fragment rows, an ingested blob that nothing names is indistinguishable from a stranger's pooled blob, so it keeps its own retention day (`min(day(E) + 1, today + 8)`), counts in `POOL_CAP` meanwhile (it can evict honest pooled blobs under a flood) and its file stays. §11.1 says "`MediaObjects` deletes the unnamed blobs in that path too" and T25 says no file of the object after `delete_at_minute` + lag + reconcile lag; **for this path both hold of the sealed blobs only.** What stays is ciphertext with **no key anywhere** (`key_env` went with the object's row), for at most eight days: a seized device shows which relay blobs, by hash, and how many were received from a deleted contact. **Evidence:** `MediaStoreScenarioTest.aDeletedContactLeavesItsIngestedBlobsInThePoolForAtMostTheRetention` — a verified incoming object (17 ingested blobs, each with a file), then `DELETE FROM dm_contact`: `collectUnnamedSealed` deletes 0, `collectExpired` 0 until the retention day has passed, 17 rows and 17 files stay and count as pooled; at day + 9 the collector and the reconcile take 17 and 17. **Options.** (A) *recommended, S8:* every path that deletes a contact, a conversation or a message first calls `MediaObjects.deleteObjectsOfMessages` with every message id it takes — the call SWEEP makes —, in the same transaction (`ContactSteps.deleteContact` of `:messaging` ends in `ContactStore.deleteContact`, a bare `DELETE FROM dm_contact`, today); the second half of the test shows that it erases all 17 at once; a test in `:messaging` pins the call (a gate cannot: it is a data flow). | 27 | finding → declared | §10.4, §11.1, §12.2 (AD-8), L9-M6, T25 | **F-S3-6. An unlinked ciphertext file leaves its write and deletion times in the freed inode, so "nothing of the object after the lags" was overstated** (first lens 3; reproduced). **Measured** on ext4 (a 64 MiB image without a journal, loop-mounted `noatime` in WSL 2 Ubuntu 24.04, kernel 6.18.33.2, e2fsprogs 1.47.0, 2026-10-05) by the store's own sequence — temp file, `mtime` and `atime` set to 0, `fsync`, `rename`, directory `sync` — and then `rm`: before the unlink `atime` = `mtime` = 0, `ctime` = the write time, birth time = the write time (nanoseconds); after it `debugfs stat <inode>` of the freed inode shows **`crtime` = the write time, `dtime` = the unlink time, `ctime` = `mtime` = the unlink time** (the kernel's truncate overwrote the zeroed `mtime`) and `atime` 0; and the **directory's** `mtime` and `ctime` are the time of the last write or unlink in `m/`, never zeroed. A seized ext4 device therefore keeps each object's write time and each file's deletion time until the inode is reused (the review counted 17 to 805 adjacent inodes per object). **An overwrite of the contents first (the brief's request, row 11) would change none of it,** and `setTimes(0, 0)` on `m/` after each sync would zero the directory's `mtime` but not its `ctime`. **Declared:** L9-M6 and the AD-8 cell of §12.2's "media expires" row now say so; T25's "no file of the object" is a statement about names and contents, not about inodes; row 11's claim stays what it was. **Not measured, for S0b:** f2fs (its own inode times and `inode_crtime`) and how soon a phone's `/data` reuses freed inodes, which is the only thing that ends the residue. **Option, for the weighing when Q18 is revisited:** a preallocated arena file with fixed slots hides the per-object times at the cost of a slot allocator and in-place overwrites; it is not in Phase 11. |

The other comparisons of the migration were read for the same shape: a NULL operand either aborts through a `NOT NULL` that has no default or is guarded by `IS NOT` or `COALESCE`. **Outside S3 (§20, F-M11):** the same shape is in Phase 9's `dm_conversation_timer` (`timer_version` 5 → 0) and `dm_pair_offer_root` (`acknowledged` 1 → 0), reproduced on the v8 schema; a separate task. |

The four were the table name from a template (`FROM $OBJECTS`), the table name from a constant joined with `+`, `UPDATE media_object … RETURNING key_env` and `DELETE FROM media_blob … RETURNING file_name`. **Fix:** `DmExpiredRowLint.readQueriesOverExpiringRows(…, strict = true)`, used by the media lint only (the DM and channel lints call the default mode and see what they saw): a write with a `RETURNING` clause is a read; a constant of the **same file** (`const val X = "…" + "…"`) is substituted into `$X`, `${X}` and `+ X +`, in front of, between and after the literals; a table position the scan cannot resolve (`FROM $t`, or a literal that ends at `FROM` or `JOIN` and goes on with an expression) is itself a finding, so it fails until the DAO spells the table out. **Tests:** `MediaExpiredRowLintTest` — the fixture tree gained `Templated.kt` with the four shapes, the unresolved ones and the decoys that must stay unreported (a derived table of other tables, a write with no `RETURNING`); the default scan finds none of the nine new shapes (it finds only the read that spells its table out, beside them in the same file) and the strict one all nine; one mutant per half (`RETURNING`, constants, unresolved positions) is red. **Still not seen, and written in the headers of both lints:** a constant declared in another file, a name computed by a call, and the subqueries of a write. **Judged, the one read of an expiring media table inside a write that decides a value:** `MediaFragments.insertIncomingRow` (`INSERT … SELECT … EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM media_blob …)`) binds a fragment to a blob row whatever its retention day, so a late descriptor adopts a pooled blob past its last day while `MEDIA-GC` has not collected it, and waits for the blob after it has. That is what the schema's `media_fragment_bound_blob_insert` requires (a row for a blob that exists is `bound`), the outcomes differ by when the maintenance ran and by nothing a relay sees (T47), and the first is the better one: **accepted**, pinned by `MediaFragmentsTest.aLateDescriptorAdoptsAPooledBlobPastItsDayUntilTheCollectorHasRunAndWaitsForTheBlobAfterIt`. |
